14:Tb60,## Context

`.github/workflows/voice-live-e2e.yml` is the only genuinely-real scheduled voice lane (Railway contract + fused ASR/Kokoro + optional mixed round-trip + acoustic attribution matrix). MVP workstream 7 relies on it as the standing proof that the cloud voice services and the local round-trip path stay healthy. It is currently red every night, which makes a real Railway or round-trip regression indistinguishable from the known failure.

## What exists today (verified read-only via gh, 2026-07-22)

- `gh run list --workflow voice-live-e2e.yml`: conclusions failure (2026-07-22), failure (07-21), failure (07-20), cancelled (07-19), cancelled (07-18), cancelled (07-17).
- In the latest run 29907610867: `Railway Kokoro/Whisper live contract` SUCCESS, `Real fused ASR + optional mixed round-trip (self-hosted)` SUCCESS, `Canonical voice matrix index` SUCCESS, but `Real acoustic VAD + diarization + self-voice matrix (self-hosted)` FAILURE (job 88882702314; `--log-failed` tail shows only checkout/cleanup noise, so the failing step needs on-runner triage). The four device lanes (macOS/Windows Electrobun, iOS, Android) are permanently skipped env-gated jobs.
- The three cancelled runs (07-17..19) predate the failures and suggest runner contention or the `cancel-in-progress` concurrency group killing scheduled runs — worth confirming while triaging (same starvation class as #16741/#16901, but this workflow's failing job is distinct and untracked).
- No open issue tracks this: searched the LifeOps board dump and the full open-issue index for voice-live/acoustic/nightly — only #13397 (needs-human acoustic stress evidence, unrelated) and #16167 (develop test-lane regressions, different workflow).

## What is missing

- Root cause of the acoustic-matrix job failure on the self-hosted runner (model/ABI/credential in require-real mode? runner provisioning drift? real regression?).
- Explanation/fix for the 07-17..19 cancellations so scheduled coverage is not silently lost.
- Three consecutive green (or green-with-designed-red-canary) scheduled runs after the fix.

## Acceptance criteria (evidence inline, per repo Definition of Done)

- [ ] Failing step identified with the actual error from the runner, pasted in a `<details>` block (not the checkout noise `--log-failed` returns today).
- [ ] Fix PR (workflow, provisioning doc, or code) linked; if the cause is runner provisioning, the provisioning change is recorded in the workflow header comment / #9454 handoff notes, not applied silently.
- [ ] Links to >=3 subsequent scheduled runs where every non-env-gated job is green; the Railway contract job stays green throughout.
- [ ] If any job is downgraded/skipped as part of the fix, the skip is loud (never green-skip) and the rationale is documented in the workflow file.

Part of the LifeOps MVP program — see packages/docs/ongoing-development/mvp/MVP.md (workstream 7).15:T580,Found during review of #16977.

Pool containers are provisioned under the sentinel `WARM_POOL_ORG_ID`, so `prepareManagedElizaBaseEnvironment` mints their `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_API_KEY` (via `apiKeysService.createForAgent`) for the POOL org/user. `claimWarmContainer` transfers the DB row to the claiming user's org, and #16977 transfers the boot-coupled inbound auth keys — but the RUNNING container's outbound cloud identity (`ELIZAOS_CLOUD_API_KEY` in its process env) still belongs to the pool org until the next container restart re-materializes env from the row.

Consequences in the window between claim and first restart:
- cloud-API usage from the container (inference routing, usage tracking) attributes to the pool org, not the claiming org — billing/limits skew;
- revoking the claiming org's keys does not cut the container's cloud access, and vice versa.

Possible directions: mint the agent API key at claim time and include it in the post-claim push path (needs a container-side route to swap `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_API_KEY` live), or rotate via a fast restart out-of-band after claim, or re-attribute pool-key usage rows at claim time. Needs design — the claim path's latency budget is the whole point of the warm pool.

Refs: #16977 review comment, `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/managed-eliza-config.ts`, `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/agent-sandboxes.ts` (claimWarmContainer).16:Tdd7,## Gap from #16615 (merged)

### [high] Empty-final turn (silence/noise EOT) strands the client in 'thinking' — the new invariant's untrue edge

packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/session/lib/session.ts:478-487 — commitTurn sends {t:"stt_final", text: ""} then finishTurn (usage only, session.ts:676-693, no speaking_start/end frame) and returns WITHOUT dispatching the LLM leg; server state resets to 'listening'. The client machine (packages/ui/src/voice/voice-session-state.ts:142-153) maps every stt_final to phase 'thinking' unconditionally — the new comment claims stt_final is the request-dispatch edge, which is false for empty finals. The only exits from 'thinking' are speaking_end/interrupted/fatal-error (packages/ui/src/voice/voice-session-client.ts:451-490; 'usage' at 486-489 is a no-op), so after a noise-triggered turn (StartOfTurn from a cough + 5s eot_timeout → EndOfTurn with empty transcript, the exact case the server comment documents) the status bar shows 'Thinking' indefinitely, the composer reports the mic off while capture is still hot (chat-view-hooks.tsx:1027-1037 excludes 'thinking' from isListening), and recovery requ

**Fix direction:** In applyServerEvent, treat an empty-text stt_final as a completed turn (e.g. phase 'complete' so the client loops to listening) or have the server send a turn-terminal frame (speaking_end or a dedicated turn_done) on the empty-final path; add a machine test for stt_final with text "".

### [medium] Mic tap is a silent no-op during the 'thinking' phase — stop control lost for the window this PR lengthened

packages/ui/src/components/composites/chat/chat-composer.tsx:345-355 routes a mic tap to stopListening only when voice.isListening; otherwise startListening. Post-PR isListening excludes 'thinking' (packages/ui/src/components/pages/chat-view-hooks.tsx:1027-1037), so a tap during thinking reaches ChatView.tsx:439-448 handleMicStartListening, whose barge-in branch requires voiceSession.agentSpeaking (false during thinking) and falls through to beginVoiceCapture → voiceSession.start() → realtime.start(), which returns {kind:"unavailable"} because clientRef.current is set (packages/ui/src/hooks/useRealtimeVoiceSession.ts:438-440). Net: during the entire EOT+TTFT window (which this PR deliberately extended by ~850ms plus full model TTFT) the mic button is dead — pre-PR isListening was true in 'thinking' so the same tap hit endVoiceCapture and stopped the session. The tap also side-effects stopSpeaking() and overwrites voiceDraftBaseInputRef (chat-view-hooks.tsx:959-975). Voice barge-in stil

**Fix direction:** In handleMicStartListening (ChatView.tsx), when voiceSession.realtimeActive and status is 'thinking', route the tap to voiceSession.bargeIn() (client bargeIn is not phase-gated, voice-session-client.ts:819-827, and server interrupt() aborts the in-flight LLM for a thinking turn) or to endVoiceCapture; add a hook test for a tap in the thinking phase.

## Design choice to make (either satisfies)

- Client-side: treat an empty-text `stt_final` as turn-complete (loop to listening), or
- Server-side: emit a turn-terminal frame (`speaking_end` or a dedicated `turn_done`) on the empty-final path.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A noise-triggered turn (StartOfTurn from a cough → empty transcript EOT) returns the client to listening — never an indefinite 'Thinking'
- [ ] Mic tap remains a functional stop control during 'thinking'
- [ ] Machine test for `stt_final` with `text: ""`

### Real Cloud flow

- [ ] Fresh launch shows only the launch-approved Eliza Cloud onboarding path.
- [ ] Complete a real supported external-browser auth round trip; no dev-login, fabricated callback, or copied token.
- [ ] Return without a login loop, blank WebView, or onboarding restart.

- [ ] Exercise home, launcher/widgets, chat, and notifications where supported.
- [ ] Force-stop/cold relaunch; the same session, agent, and history restore without onboarding replay.
- [ ] Capture filtered logcat, WebView console/network evidence, backend/provisioning logs, screenshots, and MP4.
- [ ] Confirm no crash, ANR, unhandled WebView error, protected pre-auth probe, or secret appears in evidence.

## One-hour demo runbook

1. Keep the proven iOS Simulator installed/authenticated; do not reset, rebase, or rebuild it immediately before the demo.
2. Connect/unlock the LP3, enable USB debugging, and approve this Mac.
3. Build and guarded-install the Cloud-only debug APK from the exact source above.
4. Use one known-funded staging account and one known-running agent.
5. Demonstrate: cold launch → restored session → home → live nonce chat → notifications/history.
6. Keep the LP3 charged, awake, adb-authorized, and on reliable networking.
7. Preserve reviewed fallback recordings/screenshots; use the live flow when healthy.
8. Do not merge, redeploy, rotate credentials, or modify fleet capacity during the demo window.

18:T142a,## Context

MVP workstream 6 acceptance bar (packages/docs/ongoing-development/mvp/MVP.md, 'View-chat' row): a real-LLM scenario changes each targeted view's state — 'turn on voice', 'delete that document', 'reset my background' — *without* the model emitting a raw selector. Background is covered (`packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios/background-live.scenario.ts`, live-only, asserts BACKGROUND routing + a `background:apply` broadcast payload ledger; plus `plugins/plugin-app-control/test/scenarios/background-shader-undo-redo.scenario.ts`). The other two named targets have **no scenario anywhere in the repo**, and their implementing issues (#14364, #14365) are closed, so both residuals are untracked.

## Target 1 — 'turn on voice' (SETTINGS voice section)

What exists (verified on origin/develop):
- The semantic write path is fully built: `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/settings.ts` (SETTINGS action, #14364) carries a complete voice section — `VoiceSettingsPrefs` (`continuous: off|vad-gated|always-on`, VAD auto-stop prefs), `VOICE_SETTINGS_APPLY_EVENT` broadcast, and the #14910 twin invariant (chat-issued voice writes seed the exact defaults the Voice settings UI uses, so the two write paths cannot diverge).
- Five live-only SETTINGS scenarios exist as templates in `plugins/plugin-app-control/test/scenarios/` — canonical shape `settings-shell-toggle.scenario.ts`: loopback API captures the real backend write; finalChecks assert `selectedAction: SETTINGS` + `actionCalled SETTINGS success` + the exact captured request body (behavior, never phrasing). ## Safety

No manual DB write, global image pin, ad-hoc systemd override/restart, or rollback bypass. The failed job is rollback-safe/pre-cutover.1a:T1005,## Summary

Dedicated-agent provisioning is running the **cold path** in prod (~30–120s typical, up to ~11 min worst case) because the **warm pool is empty**: `WARM_POOL_ENABLED` is not set on the `eliza-provisioning-worker` daemon host, so `WarmPoolManager.replenish()` early-returns a no-op and the pool never refills. The replenish **wiring** was already fixed and deployed (#15310 / `b17fbbc7048`, in prod since the 2026-07-13 promote) — this is the remaining **config half**.

cc @0xSolace — your warm-pool lane (you authored the replenish fix). Diagnosed via read-only prod inspection (daemon journal + DB). **Zero code needed — two config settings.**

## Verified evidence

- `warmPoolEnabled()` defaults **false** — `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/config/containers-env.ts:647-651` (`raw === "true" || raw === "1"`).
- `WARM_POOL_ENABLED` is set truthy **nowhere in the repo or deploy config** — not in `wrangler.toml` `[vars]`/`[env.staging.vars]`/`[env.production.vars]`, not in any workflow. Read/tested only.
- On the provisioning-worker daemon host it is **absent from the daemon EnvironmentFile** (grep = 0, incl. the two most recent backups) → `replenish()` is a silent no-op (`agent-warm-pool.ts` → `"WARM_POOL_ENABLED=false (no-op)"`), and the log line is gated on `created>0||failed>0`, so nothing surfaces.
- **Prod DB: zero warm-pool rows** (`agent_sandboxes` has no `pool_status` entries). The Worker-side claim (`.../provision/route.ts:172`) therefore returns null and cold-paths regardless.
- The break is confessed in-code at `packages/scripts/cloud/admin/daemons/provisioning-worker.ts:1038-1046` ("THIS IS THE MISSING CALLER…"). The wiring fix `b17fbbc7048` is deployed; the enable flag most plausibly lived only in the retired control-plane sidecar's env and was never carried onto the daemon host.

## Fix — two settings, both required (A fills, B claims)

**A) Daemon (fills the pool):** set `WARM_POOL_ENABLED=true` in the provisioning-worker daemon's EnvironmentFile + restart → `processPoolReplenishCycle` (`provisioning-worker.ts:1730-1747`) fills within one infra-maintenance cycle (burst-limited to 3/cycle). Add the key to the daemon deploy workflow's env reconcile list so a control-plane re-arm can't silently drop it again (the #15160 drift class).

**B) Worker (claims at provision time):** add `WARM_POOL_ENABLED = "true"` as a **committed var** in `wrangler.toml [env.production.vars]` + deploy — **committed, not a dashboard secret** (`wrangler.toml:533-538` documents dashboard secrets getting wiped on Worker redeploys, which already silently re-closed apps-deploy once and is the likely cause of the original disappearance).

Either half alone leaves provisioning cold.

## Adjacent follow-ups (not the pool fix)

- The provisioning-worker daemon OOM-restarts under a 1024MB heap cap (frequent `Reached heap limit` fatals, clustered in ~6-hourly storms). Separate reliability issue — raise the heap and persist the mitigation out of ephemeral `/run/systemd`. It can intermittently trip the fail-closed heartbeat check and 503 creates, but it is **not** why the pool is empty (cycles complete between restarts and still create nothing with the flag off).
- `WarmPoolManager.healthCheck()` and `rollout()` have **no live caller** anywhere — stuck (10-min) and stale-image pool entries are never reaped. Consider wiring `healthCheck()` into a daemon phase.

Half A is a prod ops action (env + restart; money surface — worth a sizing nod on how many warm containers to hold). Half B is a Worker PR + promote (your lane; carries the dashboard-secret-wipe redeploy risk). Suggested order: **A** (verify the pool fills via journal `warm pool replenish cycle complete` + DB `pool_status` rows) → **B** → confirm a test dedicated create claims warm (seconds instead of minutes).

Happy to do Half A and open the Half-B `wrangler.toml` PR if you want it off your plate — your call on the lane.

1. Two conversations used in parallel are not state-isolated. A queued/in-flight turn is correctly pinned to its target conversation for transport, but optimistic/streaming UI mutations still write through the single active `conversationMessages` state and can project a reply into the transcript selected later.
2. Reloading while a send is pending can leave the composer in a sending/stop state for more than 120 seconds instead of recovering from server truth within a bounded interval.

## Root cause / scope

`useChatSend` pins `conversationId` at enqueue (#10753), but `runQueuedChatSend` paints optimistic rows and stream/failure/terminal updates through unscoped `setConversationMessages`. Conversation identity is checked only around some history reloads. Navigation or a second conversation can therefore receive UI mutations owned by the first turn.

The reload path has no durable pending-turn recovery contract. A navigation reload tears down the client request, while the server turn may continue; the new page must start with an unlocked composer and reconcile the selected conversation rather than inheriting or recreating an unbounded pending state.

## Acceptance

- [ ] Parallel sends/navigation never project conversation A's optimistic row, tokens, terminal state, or reload into conversation B.
- [ ] A reload during a pending send restores an operable composer and preserves or settles the user turn from server truth within a bounded interval.
- [ ] Deterministic hook/component tests pin both races.
- [ ] Real Chromium staging evidence covers two parallel conversations and reload-during-send.
- [ ] No auth, API-key validation, pinned pre-stream HTTP 402 billing, or SSE-auth ordering changes.
- [ ] No warm-send median regression.

## Coordination

#16629 owns cold scope hydration only. This issue intentionally avoids its auth/shared-runtime files and any send prewarm changes.
23:Tbbf,## Incident

On staging after fleet image recreation, a dedicated agent's process and HTTP health endpoint remained green for nearly two hours while its local PGlite adapter was terminally closed.

Authoritative runtime errors repeated continuously:

- `PGlite is closed`
- `Database is shutting down - operation rejected`
- SQL retries exhausted after 3 attempts
- task queue queries failed with `TASK_QUERY_FAILED`

Docker reported `Up ... (healthy)`, the control plane reported `running` with a current heartbeat and zero errors, and no automated recovery fired. Restarting the one container immediately restored database, conversation, and authenticated streamed-chat operations. This is a production-promotion blocker because a false-green agent cannot serve any DB-backed interaction.

Incident receipt: `projects/eliza-fleet/STAGING-INCIDENT-2026-07-18-EVENING.md` (operator-side evidence; 2026-07-18 22:21Z through 2026-07-19 00:12Z).

## Scope

Implement an end-to-end liveness contract for terminal database closure:

1. Agent readiness/liveness must exercise the database, not infer health from process/runtime state alone.
2. A terminal PGlite closed/shutdown result must make liveness unhealthy.
3. The provisioning/supervisor path must consume that unhealthy result and restart/recover the agent/container.
4. Bounded transient DB errors must not cause restart loops. Distinguish terminal closure from transient probe failures, apply backoff, and enforce a restart budget/cooldown.
5. Preserve auth, billing, local-state durability, and existing persistence/restore behavior.

## Reproduction and proof required

- Close a real PGlite adapter after successful boot.
- Prove readiness/liveness flips red while the process remains alive.
- Prove the supervisor recovery path fires for terminal closure.
- Prove transient probe failures do not immediately restart and repeated recovery is budgeted/backed off.
- Focused agent health and provisioning-worker tests, plus exact endpoint payload/state-transition evidence.

## Block-0 duplicate check

Searched open issues, open PRs, and Project 12 for `PGlite is closed`, DB health/liveness/readiness, and auto-restart. Related but non-duplicative:

- #16309 / #16311 cover fresh-boot service/schema/scheduler failures, not a post-boot terminally closed adapter or supervisor recovery.
- #15228 covers stale pid-1 lock crash loops where the process exits/restarts, not a live process with false-green DB health.
- #15603 and #15783 cover hosted-agent hardening and off-box durability, not this liveness failure.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] DB-exercising readiness/liveness reports terminally closed PGlite as unhealthy.
- [ ] Supervisor/provisioning consumes the signal and performs bounded recovery.
- [ ] Terminal vs transient classification is explicit and tested.
- [ ] Backoff plus restart budget prevents hot restart loops.
- [ ] Real-PGlite close reproduction and recovery-path tests pass.
- [ ] No auth, billing, or durability weakening.

27:T1136,## Summary

Migrate Eliza's native typechecking from the retired `@typescript/native-preview` / `tsgo` path to stable TypeScript 7, while retaining TypeScript 6 only for compiler-API consumers that cannot migrate until the TypeScript 7 API is available.

TypeScript 7.0.2 became stable on 2026-07-08. Microsoft now publishes the native compiler as `typescript` with the `tsc` executable and recommends `@typescript/typescript6` for side-by-side API compatibility.

## Current state

- Root declares `@typescript/native-preview: ^7.0.0-dev.20260421.2` and `typescript: ^6.0.3`.
- The lock resolves native preview `7.0.0-dev.20260628.1` and TypeScript `6.0.3`.
- About 223 of 256 effective workspace typechecks (~87%) already use native `tsgo`, including Cloud API, routing, shared, SDK, and plugin-elizacloud.
- About 32 effective checks remain on legacy `tsc`; several are deliberate compatibility exceptions, while others are nested workspaces not currently audited.
- At least 26 repository consumers import or require the TypeScript 6 compiler API. A blind root `typescript` bump would break AST tooling, tenant-scope checks, runtime transpilation fallbacks, declaration workflows, and peer dependencies.
- Stable TypeScript 7 exposes `tsc`, not `tsgo`, so removing native-preview without migrating scripts would break more than 220 workspace commands.

## Why

- Use the supported stable compiler instead of a June preview build.
- Receive stable TypeScript 7 fixes, LSP behavior, and release support.
- Preserve the native compiler's material local/CI speedup. Microsoft reports typical 8–12x full-build improvements and lower aggregate memory versus TypeScript 6.
- Make compiler selection explicit and reproducible instead of relying on an indefinitely retained preview package.

This does **not** improve application, Worker, Cerebras, or gateway runtime latency; it improves compiler, CI, and editor feedback time.

## Safe migration shape

1. Preserve the TypeScript 6 API under the official compatibility package/alias, for example:

   ```json
   {
     "devDependencies": {
       "@typescript/native": "npm:typescript@^7.0.2",
       "typescript": "npm:@typescript/typescript6@^6.0.2"
     }
   }
   ```

2. Replace standard native-check commands from `tsgo` to the stable TypeScript 7 `tsc` binary, without redirecting API-dependent tooling away from the TypeScript 6 alias.
3. Inventory and migrate package-local TypeScript declarations in waves; the repo currently contains TS 5.x, TS 6.0.x, and root-hoisted consumers.
4. Audit TypeScript 7 hard errors/default changes (`baseUrl`, legacy module resolution, ES5/downlevel emit, `types`, `rootDir`, strict/module defaults, and JS/JSDoc differences).
5. Byte/API-compare declaration output for core packages and all dist-path consumers before changing emit commands.
6. Run Linux, macOS, and Windows validation for root typecheck/build/pack, Cloud Worker dry-runs, and editor/LSP behavior.
7. Benchmark warm/cold wall time and peak memory for root and Cloud typechecks. Tune `--checkers` and Turbo concurrency together; multiple native compilers already exhausted 16 GB CI runners in prior work.
8. Keep a documented TypeScript 6 rollback path until TypeScript 7.1's compiler API and peer ecosystem are validated.

## Related cleanup

- Fix the compiler-model audit to discover every declared workspace rather than only top-level packages and one nested level.
- Re-evaluate stale exceptions: personal-assistant now passes native checking, and plugin-streaming already emits with `--noCheck`.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Stable TypeScript 7 is pinned and locked project-locally; no global compiler is required.
- [ ] All intended native typechecks use stable `tsc` and no scripts depend on the retired `tsgo` binary.
- [ ] TypeScript 6 API consumers resolve through an explicit compatibility alias/package.
- [ ] Full workspace discovery/audit passes with every exception documented.
- [ ] Root verify, full CI, Cloud tests/Worker dry-run, dist consumers, package/runtime export checks, and declaration comparisons pass.
- [ ] Before/after wall-time and peak-memory results are attached for representative local and CI runs.
- [ ] Editor setup and rollback guidance are documented.

## References

- https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-7-0/
- https://github.com/microsoft/typescript-go/blob/main/CHANGES.md

28:Tba2,## Problem

Two scheduled **staging** health workflows never actually execute because they park in the `staging` environment's required-reviewer approval gate and get superseded (shown as `cancelled`) by the next day's scheduled run:

- **Managed Dedicated Staging Canary** (`.github/workflows/managed-dedicated-canary.yml`, cron `17 8 * * *`)
- **Headscale API Key Health** (`.github/workflows/headscale-api-key-health.yml`, cron `23 7 * * *`)

Both use `environment: staging`, which has required-reviewer protection (`standujar`, `lalalune`, `NubsCarson`, `0xSolace`; `prevent_self_review: false`). Nobody approves the daily *schedule* trigger, so each run sits `waiting` until the next day's run replaces it. Net effect: **the daily health signal is non-executing by policy, not by infra.**

### Evidence (staging, read-only-to-reserved, self-cleaning canaries)

- Headscale API Key Health: last real execution **Jul 16** (`success`). Jul 17–21 all `cancelled`/`waiting`. When manually approved today (in-policy staging reviewer), it passed in **4s** — key is healthy. The multi-day "red" was pure approval starvation, masking the true signal.
- Managed Dedicated Canary: recent history all `cancelled`/`skipped`; the only executions in this window are manual approvals. A manual-approved dispatch today reached verdict **pass, 2/2 chat paths** — the dedicated path is healthy; the earlier single `rpc_bridge_unreachable` was a transient on a just-provisioned node (WireGuard NAT/tailnet route not yet warmed within the short bridge-poll window).

## Impact

- Real staging regressions in the dedicated/mesh path can sit undetected for days because the canary that would catch them never runs.
- An actually-expired Headscale key would not surface until provisioning fails in prod, because the probe that guards it is starved.

## Proposed fix (pick one; not editing workflows here per scope)

1. **Drop required-reviewer for these two bounded, self-cleaning staging health checks.** Both are read-only-to-reserved: the canary creates exactly one `alwaysOn` agent it then deletes and does not touch the reserved-grant bucket; the Headscale probe only reads the daemon-local user list. Neither warrants a human gate on the *scheduled* trigger.
2. **Scheduled auto-approver:** a tiny job (or GitHub environment "wait timer = 0 + no reviewers for `schedule` events") that approves only `schedule`-triggered deployments for these two workflows, leaving `workflow_dispatch`/PR gated.
3. **Split environment:** move these health probes to a `staging-health` environment with no required reviewer, keeping deploy-capable `staging` gated.

Recommendation: option 1 or 3. The daily lane must actually execute for the signal to mean anything.

## Non-goals

- No change to deploy-capable `staging` approvals for real deployments.
- No secret rotation implied — Headscale key is confirmed healthy.

2a:T861,## Problem

The production Docker smoke on `develop` crashes during startup with an uncaught `write EPIPE` from `runWorkflowWithSmithers` while seeded workflows start:

- Run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29954836153
- Job: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29954836153/job/89041403450
- Throw site: `plugins/plugin-workflow/src/services/smithers-runtime.ts:569`

The Smithers worker payload uses dedicated file descriptor 3 so workflow definitions and trigger data do not enter the worker environment. The parent currently writes and ends that pipe before it has attached payload-stream, process, and stderr handlers. If the worker exits before consuming FD3, the stream emits an unhandled `EPIPE`, taking down the agent process instead of returning an observable workflow failure.

Repair only the FD3 early-child-close path. Preserve the dedicated pipe and restricted worker environment. The separate inbox migration ordering failure in the same Docker log is explicitly out of scope.

Part of the LifeOps MVP program — see packages/docs/ongoing-development/mvp/MVP.md (workstream 7).2e:T726,## Bug

On the Light Phone III, a real finger drag from Home toward Apps reveals a few pixels of the next page and then snaps back. Mouse/mirror drags can pass, so desktop-only proof hides the regression.

## Root cause

The device bundles Android WebView 113. Tailwind 4 emits `touch-pan-y` through custom properties with empty slots; this WebView accepts the declaration but computes the final `touch-action` to `auto`. Android therefore claims the horizontal gesture and sends `pointercancel` after roughly 8 CSS pixels. The existing pager also commits deliberate slow drags only after 50% of the viewport and permanently classifies an ambiguous first sample as vertical.

## Scope

- Physical LP3 validation is coordinated with the existing device-owning task after review/merge; this issue does not authorize another install or app-data mutation.32:T1529,## Summary

A live prod-fleet audit (2026-07-24) found **44 dedicated agents stuck in a terminal `error` state**, spread across **42 distinct organizations** — and **37 of those orgs have no other running agent** (their *only* agent has been dead for ~11 days). This is not accumulated cruft: all 44 were healthy agents killed by a single event.

## What happened (root cause)

**All 44 errored on the same day — 2026-07-13, in a rolling window 04:00–18:00 UTC** (2–5/hour), even though they were *created* across 2026-07-07…13. The trigger was a **bad image: `ghcr.io/elizaos/eliza:sha-226a22e`**:

- **41 of 44** were on `sha-226a22e` (23/25 of the upgrade failures targeted it; 18/19 of the provisioning failures carried it).
- The reconciler moved agents onto `sha-226a22e` over ~14h and the image **never came up serving** (`agent not serving` / `Agent not running`).
- Two terminal outcomes resulted:
  - **25 × `Upgrade permanently failed after 3 attempts (agent not serving)`** — the AGENT_UPGRADE exhausted its retries.
  - **19 × `Agent was stuck in provisioning state with no active provisioning job`** — these were mid-provision onto `sha-226a22e` when their provisioning job died (consistent with the provisioning-worker heap abort fixed in #17154).
- `error_count = 0` and `database_error = ∅` on all 44 → no active retry loop, not a DB-level fault.

**Why they were never recovered:** the existing rollback/re-arm path (`buildUpgradeFailureMarker` / the `upgrade-failed-target` trailer in `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/provisioning-jobs.ts`) only covers a **rollback-safe** failure — *the new image fails but the OLD container still serves*, so the agent is re-armed on the next published target. These 44 hit a **different** class: **`agent not serving` with `previous_docker_image = ∅`** (no recorded prior image to fall back to). That case is marked permanent-`error` and then **stranded forever** — nothing rolls it back, nothing re-provisions a permanent-error agent, and (for the 19) the provisioning job that would have finished it was already dead.

## Blast radius / containment

- **Contained.** No error rows are newer than ~7 days; the current 91 running dedicated agents are healthy on a good image; nothing is still landing on `sha-226a22e`.
- **Customer impact is real and ongoing (availability):** 42 orgs affected, **37 with their sole agent dead for 11 days** — many owners likely haven't noticed, because a dead agent produces silence, not an error surface.

## The gap to fix (durable)

An upgrade/provision that ends **`not serving` with no rollback target** must not silently strand the agent in permanent `error`. It should do one of:

1. **Roll back** to a known-good digest — but `previous_docker_image` was not recorded for these, so the recovery path had nothing to fall back to (record it on every upgrade so rollback is always possible), or
2. **Re-provision / re-arm onto the current fleet target** (a permanent-`error` agent whose failed target is now superseded is exactly the row the fleet reconciler should re-attempt), or
3. at minimum **surface it for operator recovery** (a fleet health signal for `error`-state agents older than N with no active job), instead of a silent permanent death.

The 19 provisioning-stuck agents also argue for the #17154 fix to include a **sweep that re-drives (or fails cleanly) agents left in `provisioning`/`error` with no owning job** after a worker crash, rather than leaving them terminal.

## Immediate remediation (separate from the code fix)

- **Recover** the 37 sole-agent orgs by re-provisioning onto the current good image — these are live customers whose only agent is down.
- **Triage for deletion** only the 5 orgs that already have another healthy running agent (the dead one is redundant) plus any genuinely dormant org. There is **no `delete-agent`/`reap` admin script** today — deletion must go through the real agent-deletion flow (a raw SQL delete would orphan containers, node slots, and the per-room `SharedRuntimeConversation` Durable Objects, cf. #17006).

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A failed upgrade/provision that leaves the container `not serving` records enough state (prior digest / fleet target) to **auto-recover or be re-driven**, never a silent permanent `error`.
- [ ] `previous_docker_image` / `previous_image_digest` is populated on every upgrade so rollback is always possible.
- [ ] A reconciler sweep re-attempts (or cleanly closes) `error`/`provisioning` rows with no active job older than N.
- [ ] The 37 sole-agent-down orgs from 2026-07-13 are recovered; the redundant/dormant ones are cleaned via the real deletion flow.

## Evidence

Live prod DB aggregates (2026-07-24, counts/timings/image-tags only — no org identifiers): 44 `error` agents, all `updated_at` = 2026-07-13; created 2026-07-07…13; last heartbeat 7–30d; 42 distinct orgs; 37 with no other running agent; target image `ghcr.io/elizaos/eliza:sha-226a22e` on 41/44. `docker_nodes`: 5 prod robots, capacity 24 each (120 total), 91 running dedicated → 29 free slots. DB↔container drift across the 5 robots was within reconciler tolerance (≈3).

Refs: #17154 (provisioning-worker heap abort — likely cause of the 19 stuck-provisioning), #17158 / #17149 (replacement-cleanup lifecycle-lock cluster), #17006 (DO cleanup on delete). Found by post-audit RCA. [stan-cloud]

## Scope

The canonical **runtime** version covers package-manager declarations and Bun setup used to build, test, package, or release this repository. Bun type packages are supporting compatibility signals, not runtime selectors; the root type anchors must align, while historical workspace type ranges should be inventoried and changed only where they claim the repository runtime baseline.

A purpose-built upstream Bun compatibility matrix may add `canary` only as a non-authoritative extra cell with an explicit allowlist. It may not be the default, sole, required, frozen-install, build, deploy, or release runtime. Embedded Android/RISC-V Bun binary artifacts are a separate shipping boundary and require artifact/device proof rather than a blind text replacement.

## Reliability invariant

A checked-in authoritative manifest or workflow must never depend on an unpublished, deleted, floating, mutable, or implicit runtime. Drift fails in repository tests before a workflow reaches setup.36:T9a4,## Problem

The dashboard conversation route owns synchronous web replies over SSE, while `client_chat` and related sources are internal runtime relays used for asynchronous delivery over WebSocket. Advertising those relays through `getMessageConnectors()` lets the planner select a second transport for a route-owned reply.

When the `MESSAGE` action selects `client_chat` during a synchronous web turn, the same response can reach the UI through both the normal SSE stream and a `proactive-message` WebSocket event. Comparing response text in the UI hides the ownership bug and can discard legitimate repeated messages.

## Current state after #17037

PR #17037 merged the correct core seam: `registerInternalSendHandler` keeps relays addressable through `sendMessageToTarget` without adding them to connector discovery, and deterministic runtime tests cover that distinction. The issue remains open because the merge did not provide the required exact-head live transport proof, and the touched dashboard fallback still catches any `getMessageConnectors()` failure and returns `false`. That converts a broken registry into a healthy-looking unregistered source and can reroute delivery to the dashboard.

- [x] Internal relays are not exposed as planner-selectable message connectors.

39:T1152,## Summary

Every Eliza Cloud email magic link should also contain a six-digit, single-use companion code. A user on a constrained or cross-device client such as LP3 can type the code on the existing login page while the magic link remains available and unchanged.

This is blocked on an additive Steward auth contract. Eliza is only the Steward client/proxy and does not own challenge storage or email rendering.

Signed: **[sol-orch]**

## Recon findings

- Eliza login calls `@stwd/sdk` `StewardAuth.signInWithEmail()` from `packages/ui/src/cloud/public-pages/pages/login/steward-login-section.tsx`.
- `/steward/*` is a signed upstream proxy in `packages/cloud/api/src/steward/embedded.ts`.
- The link callback is `packages/ui/src/cloud/public-pages/pages/auth/email-callback-page.tsx`, which calls `verifyEmailCallback()` and syncs the Steward session cookie.
- Steward owns `POST /auth/email/send`, `POST /auth/email/verify`, token persistence, tenant email templates, and the mail provider.
- Steward already has six-digit email OTP endpoints, but they intentionally return a short-lived `emailGrant` for passkey registration. They do not log the user in.
- Steward also has device-code approval/poll primitives, but those are a separate protocol and are not aliases for an email magic-link challenge.

Calling both existing send methods is not acceptable. It produces independent challenges, may produce two messages, cannot invalidate both credentials when either succeeds, and would broaden the passkey grant's security contract.

## Required upstream dependency

Add an additive Steward combined challenge:

1. `POST /auth/email/send` creates one challenge and sends one email containing the existing link plus a six-digit code.
2. Link-token hash and code verifier resolve to one central, purpose-bound challenge record.
3. The central record is atomically consumed, so a successful link invalidates the code and a successful code invalidates the link, including concurrent attempts.
4. Add `POST /auth/email/code/verify`, then expose an SDK method returning the normal `StewardAuthResult | StewardMfaRequiredResult`.
5. Leave `sendEmailOtp`, `verifyEmailOtp`, and `emailGrant` behavior unchanged.

Security requirements:

- server-side cryptographic random generation,
- short shared TTL, recommended 10 minutes,
- hash/keyed verifier at rest, no plaintext token/code,
- bound to normalized email, tenant, and `email-login` purpose,
- atomic single use and cross-invalidation,
- per-IP plus per-challenge/target rate limits,
- maximum five failed attempts and hard lock,
- constant-time verifier compare where applicable,
- no code, email, raw token, or full challenge alias in logs,
- no account-enumeration differences,
- normal tenant, SSO, allowed-method, and MFA gates.

## Eliza acceptance criteria after Steward SDK release

- Existing link callback remains unchanged and valid.
- The `email-sent` state includes optional copy: `Enter the 6-digit code from your email`.
- Accessible input uses `inputMode="numeric"`, `autoComplete="one-time-code"`, numeric paste sanitization, `maxLength={6}`, and an explicit label.
- Code success uses the existing `handleSuccess()` session/cookie flow.
- Explicit loading, invalid, expired, locked/too-many-attempts, and transport-error states.
- Resend has a visible cooldown and requests a fresh combined challenge.
- Existing `packages/ui` tokens and Lucide icons only, with LP3 touch sizing.
- Focused backend, SDK, email-template/provider, UI, callback regression, and LP3 viewport tests.

## Collision check

- #16368 and #16365 concern account-provider reauthentication and are separate.
- #15483 covered LP3 WebView/handoff/CORS failures and is closed. This feature only overlaps at the constrained-device UX boundary and must not absorb those bugs.
- No matching open Eliza PR or issue was found on 2026-07-14.

## Evidence

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Full Eliza initiation, proxy, callback, and session path traced | PASS |
| Steward storage, template/provider, OTP, and device-code primitives traced | PASS |
| Safe one-PR implementation available in Eliza | NO, blocked on Steward API/SDK |

3e:Td5a,## Problem

The browser PCM recorder drops every frame until the VAD detector begins buffering. `DEFAULT_LOCAL_ASR_AUTO_STOP.startGraceMs` is 250 ms, and frames below the speech thresholds are also discarded before the first detected speech frame.

Current code:
- detector: `packages/ui/src/voice/local-asr-capture.ts:189-269`
- recorder buffer gate: `packages/ui/src/voice/local-asr-capture.ts:519-529`

On an iOS/WebKit AudioContext running at 48 kHz, the current 4096-frame ScriptProcessor callback is about 85 ms. A user who starts speaking as soon as the mic becomes live can therefore lose multiple leading callbacks—the first syllable or a short first word—before full-WAV batch transcription.

An abandoned branch proposed a 200 ms bounded PCM pre-roll in commit https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/commit/76ad66201ba2c2c53e624b9bdd61e51a80e3f9ea. Do not cherry-pick that commit wholesale: it also shortened the existing 12-second utterance cap to four seconds without device evidence.

## Scope

- Keep a bounded rolling PCM buffer while auto-stop VAD has not begun buffering.
- When real speech is first detected, prepend the retained pre-roll exactly once before the first speech frame.
- Bound the ring in milliseconds using the actual AudioContext sample rate (target default: 200 ms).
- Clear it on stop, cancel, and teardown; memory must not grow with idle time.
- Apply through the shared WAV recorder so both local-inference and cloud batch ASR benefit.
- Keep the current `maxSpeechMs: 12_000` policy. A shorter cap requires separate product approval and real-device evidence.

## Non-goals

No streaming-ASR/realtime-WebSocket changes, provider/route changes, WAV-header normalization, or auto-send/UI redesign.

## Acceptance criteria

- Recorder-level tests drive fake AudioContext callbacks with identifiable pre-grace, pre-threshold, and speech samples, then decode the returned WAV and prove:
  - only the newest bounded pre-roll is retained;
  - it precedes the first detected speech frame;
  - it is flushed exactly once;
  - older frames are discarded.
- Coverage at both 16 kHz and 48 kHz proves duration-based rather than chunk-count-based bounding.
- No-speech input does not promote pre-roll into a valid utterance.
- Capture without auto-stop remains byte-for-byte behaviorally unchanged.
- Echo-gate, trailing-silence, duplicate-stop, cancel, and empty-capture behavior remain green.
- A longer than four-second spoken phrase is retained, proving the 12-second cap was not shortened.
- `bun run --cwd packages/ui test`, relevant voice tests, typecheck/Biome, and `bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app` pass.

## Required real evidence

On current installed builds, test iOS PWA and desktop Chrome with phrases whose first word starts immediately after mic activation, including short/plosive openers such as “Turn…”, “Set…”, and “Play…”.

Attach and manually review:

- before/after raw WAVs and transcripts for the same phrase set;
- evidence that the leading word is present after the change;
- a 5–8 second utterance proving it is not cut at four seconds;
- screen recording with audio;
- browser network/console logs and matching backend STT logs;
- WAV sample rate, duration, RIFF/data sizes, and first voiced-sample timing.

Related timing work: #15267 and #15276. The reverted chunk-streaming experiment #15426 is not an implementation source for this fix.3f:Te6e,## Summary

The staging chat latency harness reports **~0.39s TTFT** on build `036e36fcf` (carries #16629 + #16713), but real users feel **4-6s to first response** on the same build. Both are correct — they measure **different points on the same request**. The harness measures `curl time_starttransfer` (**first response BYTE**) on a **warm, reused conversation** with **already-hydrated auth/org/scope**. Users feel **first visible TOKEN** on a **cold browser session** hitting a **real agent** for the **first message**, which pays two stalls the harness structurally cannot observe.

## Reproduction (clean egress, build `036e36fcf`)

Isolating the pre-inference auth+scope path (404, zero inference):

- Cold key, first hit (forces cold org+scope Hyperdrive trip): **1.09s–4.43s**
- Warm (scope cached), same key: **~0.02–0.22s**
- Same valid key, 10 sequential hits to a nonexistent agent: first **1.091s**, rest **~0.225s**

Raw same-key n=10: `1.091 0.215 0.223 0.212 0.237 0.230 0.225 0.227 0.222 0.208`. The first cold hit pays the full user/org/scope round-trip; every subsequent hit is warm. This matches the R8 request-layer bimodal (med 1.23 / p95 7.77) and the felt 4-6s.

## Two stacked root causes, both invisible to `time_starttransfer`

**A. Cold auth+scope hydration (request layer, per fresh session).** `authenticateApiKeyWithOrg` + org-scoped resolve = cold Hyperdrive trips on first hit. #16629 parallelizes the two org lookups and adds a client `prewarmSharedChatScope` on composer mount, but (1) the first cold trip still costs ~1-4s even parallelized, and (2) the client prewarm fires only for direct shared Cloud agents on composer mount — a user's own long-lived agent + history route may not trip it, so it's paid inline on send.

**B. Provider-retry backoff (inference layer, per transient 5xx).** Shared Cerebras turn runs `streamText` with no `maxRetries` → AI-SDK default `maxRetries:2, initialDelayInMs:2000, backoffFactor:2`, no cross-provider fallback for Cerebras. One transient 5xx = **+2s**, two = **+6s** of pure sleep **between first byte and first token**. #16713 bounds this to 1 retry (caps at +2s) but does not eliminate it. `time_starttransfer` fires at the byte, before this sleep, so it never sees it.

## Why the harness passes and users don't (same build)

The harness measures the global minimum of the latency surface: warm scope + first-byte + stream-cancelled-after-byte-1. The tail is real and server-side; it just doesn't live where `time_starttransfer` looks.

## Proposed fixes

1. **Harness (highest leverage — it's giving false GREEN):** measure **first-token** (parse SSE to first non-empty content delta) and **full-response**, not `time_starttransfer`. Add a **cold-session** shape (fresh key/conv per probe, not 20 reused turns). Don't cancel the stream after byte 1.
2. **Cold-scope (A):** confirm #16629 client prewarm covers the own-agent + history route; if not, extend it and/or add a short-TTL server-side scope cache keyed by (apiKey, orgId). Target cold first-hit < 1s.
3. **Provider-retry (B):** investigate why Cerebras 5xxes on ~half of warm staging turns (`wrangler tail` for `RetryError` / `[OpenRouter] Primary router`); if high, bound interactive turn to 0 retries + fail-fast fallback instead of sleeping.

## Gate impact

Prod promotion should NOT gate on the 0.39s first-byte number. Re-measure first-token + full-response on a cold session and gate on warm p95 first-token < 2s AND cold first-token < 3s.

Refs: #16629, #16713. Priors: internal LATENCY-R9 (false-green first-byte 0.39s), PROD-PROMOTION-R8 (request-layer bimodal, correctly held), CHAT-LATENCY-2026-07-20, LATENCY-STALL-2026-07-20.

42:T729,## Problem
`.github/workflows/app-real-e2e.yml` defines a **single** job (`app-real-e2e`, "Browser-driven real app e2e (nightly)") with **job-level** `continue-on-error: true` at line 52:

```yaml
jobs:
  app-real-e2e:
    name: Browser-driven real app e2e (nightly)
    ...
    timeout-minutes: 60
    continue-on-error: true   # <-- line 52
```

Because it is the workflow's only job, a **job failure resolves the whole run to `success`**. Concretely, run [#29733684389](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/actions/runs/29733684389): **run conclusion = success**, but its **only job = failure**. The real browser-driven e2e signal is swallowed — a green checkmark that means nothing.

## Why it matters
This is a green-run masking a genuine browser-e2e failure. It is not currently a **required/gating** lane (advisory nightly), so it is lower-severity than a #16569-class gating vacuity — but it is exactly the "fails open" pattern tracked in #13620, and it undermines any attempt to trust App Real E2E as a health signal.

## Fix options (needs owner decision — workflow-correctness edit, do NOT weaken)
1. **Move `continue-on-error` to the specific step(s)** that are legitimately allowed to soft-fail, so the job's real conclusion surfaces; or
2. Split the soft-failing exploratory work into a separate job so the core e2e job can hard-fail; or
3. If the whole lane is intentionally advisory, keep it soft **but** surface the true job outcome (e.g. a summary/annotation) so `success` is not misread as "e2e passed."

## Scope note
Filed per the CI-HEALTH-2026-07-20 vacuous-green spot-check. Not editing the workflow here (dual-model + owner sign-off required for workflow-correctness changes). Distinct from #14357 (resurrect `app-live-e2e.yml` nightly) — that is a different workflow. Umbrella: #13620.

plugins/plugin-coding-tools/src/actions/file.ts:278 adds "LIST_FILES" to FILE's similes, but packages/agent/src/actions/files.ts:165 has carried "LIST_FILES" on the FILES action (stored-media list/get/delete) since #8970 (2026-06-22), predating this PR. Both actions coexist in the default runtime: packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza-plugin.ts:290 registers filesAction and packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza.ts:3237 loads plugin-coding-tools as a core plugin. Contexts cannot save this: packages/core/src/services/message.ts:2580-2584 documents that selected contexts are passed to retrieveActions as a weight, never a filter, so both actions are always in the catalog. Net effect: a Stage-1 hint LIST_FILES meaning "browse my stored files" (the documents-context FILES use case) now resolves to the coding-workspace FILE action at rank-0 exact, and the FILES action's own simile — deliberately added in #8970 — is dead. No test covers duplicate-simile resolution: packages/core/src/runtime/__tests__/action-retrieval.test.ts:145-202 tests parent simile, child simile, and simile-vs-parent-name hijack only.

## Impact

Both actions coexist in the default runtime; the catalog's first-writer-wins simile resolution (alphabetical parent order, `FILE` < `FILES`) silently routes `LIST_FILES` to coding-tools' FILE action, stealing the stored-media listing intent that predates the new simile (#8970). The collision guard only covers similes matching a real parent NAME, not similes claimed by two parents.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `LIST_FILES` resolves unambiguously (either dropped from FILE's similes — `FILES_LIST` already covers it — or the resolver drops any simile claimed by more than one parent, with a warn)
- [ ] A cross-action simile-uniqueness regression test pins the invariant
- [ ] Existing FILE and FILES routing tests stay green

4f:T647,**Is your feature request related to a problem? Please describe.**

`plugins/plugin-web-search` is the only `ServiceType.WEB_SEARCH` provider in
the tree, and it is Tavily-backed. Agents whose main cost driver is context
size have no in-tree alternative: every web search returns snippet-style
content, and anything deeper means a second fetch-and-clean step inside the
agent loop.

**Describe the solution you'd like**

A sibling plugin, `plugins/plugin-serpdive`, implementing the same
`IWebSearchService` contract on the SERPdive API, which returns extracted,
answer-ready page content instead of links (each result carries the actual
text of the page). Same option shapes as plugin-web-search so call sites can
swap providers without changes; one provider active per agent.

Why it may be worth having in-tree: search output is LLM input, and on a
public, replayable 1,000-question blind benchmark SERPdive returns 20.2%
fewer tokens than Tavily's default search while winning 60.7% of decided
quality duels, at the same speed
(https://github.com/edendalexis/serpdive-benchmark). Free tier is 1,000
credits/month with no card, so the plugin is testable without a paid plan.

**Describe alternatives you've considered**

- Extending plugin-web-search with a second backend: rejected, eliza's model
  is independent plugins and the Tavily plugin stays untouched.
- Keeping it out of tree: possible, but the in-tree plugin set is where
  agent developers actually discover providers.

**Additional context**

I maintain SERPdive. PR ready and linked; it touches nothing outside
`plugins/plugin-serpdive/`.
50:T99d,## Problem

The bounded production image canary can boot its blue replacement successfully and then lose that replacement before cutover. The generic replacement-cleanup sweep may select a pre-cutover blue fence while its lifecycle job is still active, and database pool/advisory-lock waits are not bounded at the PostgreSQL boundary. A JavaScript watchdog can therefore abandon its awaiter while pooled database work continues, leaving the job failed and blocking safe retries.

The latest production canary reproduced this as a healthy blue boot followed by cleanup of the blue VPN node and a failed lifecycle job. The serving generation remained intact, but Notes and Calendar could not reach the new immutable image.

## Acceptance criteria

- A background cleanup sweep must not retire replacement resources owned by a pending or in-progress exclusive lifecycle job.
- Pre-cutover cleanup candidates must observe a database-time recovery grace; post-cutover old resources remain immediately eligible.
- The inner lifecycle/row-locked cleanup path must recheck active jobs and grace after outer selection.
- Replacement workloads count toward node capacity and remain protected from orphan cleanup until durable retirement.
- Every Eliza lifecycle/admin advisory-lock transaction must have bounded lock and statement waits, and PostgreSQL pool checkout must be finite.
- Authoritative delete semantics and existing idempotency/CAS guarantees must remain intact.
- Prove the deadline with two real PostgreSQL sessions and prove the cleanup race, capacity ownership, recovery, and retry paths with deterministic tests.
- Deploy the exact reviewed fix, run one bounded canary, and verify Notes/Calendar/Cerebras/restart persistence before widening.

## Evidence plan

- **Backend logs:** sanitized canary/reconciler results, exact deployed SHA, and bounded cleanup stage/elapsed logs.
- **Database/domain artifacts:** lifecycle job result, cleanup fence/capacity state, and post-restart Simple Views state.
- **Tests:** real PostgreSQL contention plus PGlite lifecycle, cleanup TOCTOU, capacity/orphan, enqueue/delete, and recovery suites.
- **UI screenshots/video:** N/A — backend control-plane fix; physical LP3 proof is captured after the canary is healthy.
- **Real model trajectory:** N/A for the fix itself; the final device acceptance includes a real Cerebras reply.

51:T5f0,Found via hand-review of live-scenarios run 30046774252 (PR #17108 view-chat shard, scenario `live-document-delete`, native.jsonl planner row 2).

The planner injected a spurious `query:"list all"` into `DOCUMENT {action:"list"}`. In `packages/core/src/features/documents/actions.ts` (`handleList`, ~805–818) → `packages/core/src/features/documents/service.ts` (`listDocuments`, ~383), any planner-supplied `query` becomes a literal lowercase substring filter over content/title/filename, and zero matches renders the absolute statement **"No documents are available."** — conflating "no documents matched your filter" with "storage is empty". Downstream turns then rationally act on the poisoned result (the live scenario's turn 4 denied an existing document's existence).

This is the "empty must not impersonate not-found" doctrine violation: a filtered-empty result must be distinguishable from an empty store.

Fix in `handleList`: when `params.query` is non-empty and the filtered result is empty, return `No documents matched "<query>". <N> document(s) exist; retry without a query to list all.` — or auto-fallback to the unfiltered list with a no-matches-for-query preamble. Add a regression test covering query-miss vs truly-empty store.

Context: the same live-flake pattern (gpt-5-mini one-shot nondeterminism on byte-identical prompts) also exposed the settings-routing brittleness fixed in #17108's scenario wording; both flakes were triggers, but each uncovered a distinct real product brittleness.52:T62f,Every scheduled Live Scenarios run from 2026-07-12 through 2026-07-23 (12 consecutive nights) was cancelled at ~120 minutes, annotated 'The run was canceled forcefully by @github-actions[bot]'. The enforcing nightly live lane has not actually completed in that entire window — the 07-23 nightly finished all setup/build and died mid 'Run EA + connector live scenarios'.

Root cause (two independent ceilings, both too low):
1. `.github/workflows/actions-zombie-janitor.yml` force-cancels any in_progress run older than its default 120-minute max-age, and Live Scenarios is not in `ACTIONS_JANITOR_EXEMPT_WORKFLOWS` (currently only Cuttlefish + OpenAgent E1).
2. The workflow's own `timeout-minutes: 120` would kill it moments later regardless.

The nightly runs all four shards serially in one job on `ubuntu-24.04` (Hetzner fleet offline); historically successful nightlies took ~10–11 hours, so a 120-minute ceiling can never fit. (Before 07-12 the nightlies failed fast in setup; #16044's 07-11 fix got them running long enough to hit the reaper.)

Fix options:
- Exempt Live Scenarios from the janitor (or raise its max-age for this workflow) AND raise the workflow `timeout-minutes` to fit the measured serial duration; or
- Split the nightly into parallel per-shard jobs — #17108's shard architecture (per-shard artifact contract + one terminal aggregate gate) is the natural vehicle, with per-shard timeouts sized to each shard.

Evidence: `gh run list --workflow live-scenarios.yml` shows the 12-run cancelled streak; the 07-23 run log shows death mid-scenario at 120m.53:T5e8,## Context

5f:T23cc,# Relates to

Refs #16917.

This PR is one staged foundation for the umbrella issue; it does **not** close
the umbrella. Follow-up production gates are tracked in:

- #17093 — strongly consistent authorization revocation and immutable session identity
- #17094 — bounded Durable Object calls and real workerd integration coverage
- #17095 — backward-compatible accounting revisions and projections
- #17096 — staged route adoption, rollback, and live evidence

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

# Critical review outcome

The original implementation was not safe to merge. It treated positive
authorization values in eventually consistent KV as a revocation boundary,
used unbounded Durable Object calls on the request path, swallowed alarm
recovery failures, did not scope one recovery lookup by tenant, and described
mocked Durable Object tests as integration coverage.

This revision separates authorization from admission:

- `INFERENCE_AUTH_CACHE_ENABLED` is independent and defaults to `false` in
  every checked-in environment.
- With that flag off, populated positive KV entries are ignored and origin
  authorization remains authoritative.
- The exact rate/spend Durable Object admission and revisioned accounting
  foundations can soak without enabling unsafe cached authorization.
- Authorization caching must remain off until #17093 provides a strongly
  consistent revocation boundary and immutable Steward session identity.

This is therefore a staged architectural foundation, not approval to enable
the complete cache-only hot path in production.

# What changes

- Covers `/v1/chat`, `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/messages`,
  `/v1/responses`, `/v1/embeddings`, shared-agent turns, and the internal
  voice-session Eliza LLM turn.
- Adds revisioned authorization, pricing, policy, and balance projections.
- Adds per-organization Durable Objects for exact rate limiting and monetary
  leases, dispatch marking, settlement, and alarm recovery.
- Adds revision-aware, idempotent accounting and anonymous-inference recovery.
- Bounds admission-gate rate, lease, release, settlement, and dispatch calls;
  stalled admission fails closed.
- Makes alarm recovery failures observable and rethrows an aggregate failure
  so Cloudflare retries the alarm.
- Scopes recovery app lookup by both app ID and organization ID.
- Runs the production Durable Object in Miniflare/workerd with real storage and
  proves concurrent $7 leases against a $10 balance admit exactly one request.
- Adds the required Durable Object and rate-limiter bindings (with
  `new_sqlite_classes` migrations) in the default, staging, and production
  environments. Relies on the existing staging/production `CACHE_KV`
  namespaces; no KV binding is added, and the top-level dev environment
  still has no `CACHE_KV`.

Direct STT, TTS, and voice-clone media/job endpoints remain outside scope.

# Risk and rollout

**Deploy ordering:** the hot-path snapshot read selects
`organizations.balance_revision`, so migrations 0177-0181 must apply before or
with the Worker deploy (the deploy workflow's migration step already runs
first — keep that ordering). `INFERENCE_BILLING_LEDGER` flips `""` -> `"db"`
in all environments in this PR: it takes effect on staging with the next
develop deploy (the intended soak) and on production only via a later
main deploy, which stays gated on the #17096 evidence.

**Large / money path.** This changes admission, reservation, crash recovery,
settlement, affiliate payout, and balance projection. Require cloud/billing
owner review.

The warm path has serial Durable Object operations before provider dispatch.
Each client operation is bounded by a 1.5 second abort timeout. The included
benchmark is in-process and does not claim deployed Cloudflare latency.

Do not enable cached authorization or broadly adopt the route path until the
dependent issues above are complete and staging supplies rollback, latency,
revocation, recovery, and accounting evidence.

# Known limitations

- No live Cloudflare deployment, network latency, or backend-log evidence is
  claimed. #17096 owns staged activation and rollback evidence.
- KV metadata is eventually consistent. It is not a safe positive
  authorization revocation mechanism, so authorization caching is default-off.

  quarantined PGlite suite; that suite passed independently.



# Post-rebase deep review (five-dimension) — addendum

A five-dimension adversarial review (DO money path, auth-cache safety,
revisioned accounting, route integration, test realness) was run against this
branch after the rebase; every post-rebase CI failure was root-caused, and the
following landed on the branch (see the review comment for the full report):

- Sub-ledger-unit creator movements are a designed `below_ledger_unit` outcome
  (consumer charge proceeds, nothing recorded, auditable) instead of failing
  the reservation; creator-movement replays are first-committed-wins on keyed
  dedupe with identity fields strictly validated and a committed-row
  direction cross-check.
- Balance snapshot reads moved to a typed repository seam; the fail-closed
  balance tests now run against real PGlite with the real 0177 trigger and
  real corrupt values (`'NaN'::numeric`, NULL) — no mocked seams.
- Dead money-path modules removed (`createDeferredAdmissionSettler`,
  `recoverInferenceChargeViaLedger`); the DB-ledger settle now returns the
  real settlement transaction id.
- Session-path IAC cache writes are gated on `INFERENCE_AUTH_CACHE_ENABLED`
  (matching the API-key path); shared-agent session scope-cache hits
  revalidate `is_active`/org identity/org active from the lifecycle-
  invalidated user cache; cache validators are mutually exclusive.
- Streaming `onFinish` with absent usage settles at the admitted estimate,
  never $0 for delivered output; `/v1/chat` gained the stream settlement
  backstop; the messages-route terminal enqueue is guarded.

## Known limitations (tracked follow-ups)

- Shared-runtime local failures thrown before `onProviderDispatch` settle
  conservatively at the estimate; propagating a "dispatch never reached" flag
  would make zero provable (#17094).
- Negative shared-agent scope entries serve cached 401/403/404 for up to the
  30 s base TTL; mutation-side invalidation would remove that window (#17093).
- `uncollectedDebtUsd` in the gate DO only grows (no repayment interplay), and
  a corrupt-invariant alarm throw can freeze recovery for that org's leases
  until manual intervention; both are availability-direction, not money-loss,
  and belong to #17094/#17095.
- Only the lease/lease race runs under real workerd; settle/dispatch/release/
  alarm and the AnonymousChatGate DO are covered by in-memory-storage unit
  suites (#17094).

# What this does

Adds a super-admin-only, exact-agent Cloud image rollout lane for immutable cross-repository digests. The path is intentionally separate from ordinary fleet reconciliation and supports:

- actor-bound, lowercase-UUID idempotency and full-request fingerprints;
- dry-run/execute plan fingerprints with exact durable-target recovery;
- transactional lifecycle ownership, capacity reservation, image CAS, audit, and completion;
- blue/green health gates with authenticated `/api/status` plus public `/api/health`;
- bounded post-cutover cleanup that can be reclaimed after worker interruption;
- exact completed-job rollback;
- an operator workflow that verifies deployed `main`, derives a stable request ID, recovers ambiguous POSTs using GET-only polling, and writes privacy-safe evidence.

This does **not** change the global fleet image, pause reconciliation, or permit manual database writes.

# Risks and deployment gate

**Infrastructure / high impact, narrowly scoped.** An incorrect control-plane change could repoint or strand the selected agent. The implementation therefore fails closed on actor, request, plan, source image, lifecycle owner, generation, health, cleanup, and rollback identity.

Merge does not itself execute a canary. Required order after CI and review:

1. deploy exact `develop` to staging with Cloud migration/API first;
2. deploy the exact same staging SHA to the provisioning worker;
3. verify staging commit and health;
4. replay the reviewed delta in a narrow PR onto current `main`;
5. deploy production Cloud migration/API, then the exact production provisioning worker;
6. dispatch one protected production canary for the named demo agent;
7. verify health, zero failed plugins, Notes/Calendar CRUD, Cerebras response, restart persistence, and LP3 behavior;
8. use the completed upgrade job for exact rollback if any gate fails.

64:T15a1,Closes #16460

## Summary

This is a cohesive interaction and visual-stability pass across the home notification shade, continuous chat sheet, startup/auth surfaces, and local development runtime.

- Gives the notification shade shared momentum physics, endpoint resistance, stable detents, coordinated card/count/control fades, clean stack expansion/collapse, desktop clear-all confirmation, and isolated chat-vs-home gestures.
- Makes notification boot/reconnect snapshots authoritative while preserving concurrent arrivals, and tags replayed WebSocket events so one-shot UI effects are not repeated.
- Reworks chat actions, inline editing, replies, playback state, timestamps, transcription, long-draft scrolling, search, upload, and pending attachments so controls stay scoped to the correct message without unintended row or sheet movement.
- Makes message-search jumps deterministic by revealing already-loaded history before fetching and routing the jump through the canonical message scroller; the target lands in view with a neutral white highlight.
- Keeps the startup logo/name static while only status text shimmers, hides desktop pager arrows on mobile, reuses canonical cloud/device login launch surfaces, and runs the local Vite server under Node so HTTP upgrade/WebSocket proxying remains connected.

## Root causes

Notification drag physics, settle state, opacity, mounting, and glass presentation were driven by partially independent transitions. Chat action/reply/transcription controls participated in message and composer layout. Search fetched before revealing bounded history while bottom-follow could override imperative scrolling. Snapshot/replay handling could conflate reconnect state with new arrivals. The pre-React startup lockup and mounted shell used different geometry, and Bun-hosted Vite did not reliably preserve proxy upgrades.

## User impact

Notification pulls and stacks now track, settle, expand, collapse, and clear as one coherent surface. Chat controls stay minimal and stable. Older search hits reliably scroll into view. Voice/transcription and attachment workflows no longer unexpectedly open or collapse the sheet. Startup/auth transitions no longer morph or fork into duplicate UI, and mobile keeps swipe navigation without desktop arrows.

65:T21e5,## What

Prep for the full three-harness benchmark campaign (eliza + hermes + openclaw; cerebras gemma-4-31b via the Eliza Cloud proxy, then claude-opus-4-8 via the subscription gateway). Two commits:

**1. Behavioral fixes (`fix`)**
- **Proxy routing**: every cerebras-provider call now honors operator env (`CEREBRAS_BASE_URL` > `BENCHMARK_BASE_URL` > `OPENAI_BASE_URL` > provider map) instead of hardcoded `api.cerebras.ai` — fixed in the runner subprocess env, code-agent-matrix cells, eliza-adapter `_normalize_model_env`, mt_bench judge, tau-bench user-sim/judge (litellm gets an explicit `api_base`), woobench evaluator, swe_bench generated OpenCode config, acceptance gate. tau-bench's judge no longer silently degrades to substring matching on LLM failure (was fabricating healthy-looking scores from a broken judge — violates the error doctrine).
- **Loopback provider forwarder** (`orchestrator/provider_forwarder.py`): publishable hermes/openclaw native lanes reject non-loopback base URLs by contract; cohorts on a remote OpenAI-compatible endpoint now auto-start a per-cohort 127.0.0.1 forwarder (per-harness ephemeral bearer tokens, SSE passthrough, fail-closed upstream errors, no key/token logging), mirroring the subscription-gateway lifecycle. Signatures unchanged.
- **Swap-safe resume**: stale-run recovery can no longer reopen finished run groups (`finished_at IS NULL` guard); cohort resume retires the killed attempt's rows before workers start; cerebras cohorts auto-recover stale namespace-less groups at startup; new `full_campaign --assume-quota-reset` resumes past a stored `retry_at` after a subscription **account swap** (fail-closed: consumed on first still-paused resume).
- **Subscription gate cluster**: the 7 provenance/publishability tests red since #16764 (they shipped broken in that PR — verified on pristine develop in a clean worktree). Root causes: umask-dependent log modes (now `0o600` at open + `fchmod`), CPython≥3.12.1 raising the recursion limit past the deep-JSON assumption (now a deterministic depth guard, strictly stricter), and two intra-PR fixture/assertion drifts (fixtures now seal sha256-chain-v2 durable records matching what the TS gateway actually emits). All fail-closed-preserving.
- **HF publishing** (`orchestrator/hf_publish.py` + `publish-hf` CLI): exports (runs/run_groups/trajectory_turns JSONL), `VACUUM INTO` sqlite snapshots (latest-only on-disk retention), path relativization, per-run-group audit commits into the **gitignored nested results repo** at `benchmark_results/`, and `HfApi.upload_folder` publish gated behind readiness/publishability/comparability/artifact-guard validation, a secret scrub, and a host-path scan for `--public`. Private by default; token fail-closed.
- Removed the satisfied `MULTITASK_ELIZA_USAGE_FIX` gate; reconciled `docs/RESULTS_MATRIX.md` with the 49-id registry (greens `test_results_matrix_sync`).

**2. Suite cleanup (`chore`)**
Loose topic docs into `docs/`; verified-dead code deleted (registry `_monolith`, clawbench groq scripts, unreferenced trust TS mirror, openclaw-benchmark vendored upstream dirs incl. committed slide decks, ad-hoc smokes, retired shim) — the three `ralphy/` files pinned by the authored-evidence retention contract (#16296) are **retained** and its test passes; 190 stale `.gitignore` lines pruned (targets deleted in #9475; deletions-only diff, no previously-ignored files resurface); doc gaps filled (entity-voice-bench had zero docs) and stale READMEs corrected against code; registry metadata paths/descriptions fixed.

## Summary

- Load the backend only for managed Cloud agents through the existing `lean-chat` plugin profile.

- Add deterministic route, launcher, Docker dependency, visual-review, and plugin behavior coverage.

## Architecture boundary

Managed dedicated Cloud agents load the runtime package through `ELIZA_PLUGIN_SET=lean-chat`. Native clients receive the Notes and Calendar renderers in the signed app bundle and expose them only while Cloud is active. Local and remote agent defaults are unchanged.

## Exact-package evidence gate

This PR remains intentionally draft while the exact-head GitHub jobs build and install the real Linux artifacts. Local structural tests are not represented as installed-package proof. The current exact-head runs are:

- [Debian amd64 + arm64 build/verification](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29888144323)
- [Snap amd64 + arm64 build/verification](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29888144271)
- [Flatpak build/verification](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29888144306)
- [packaging configuration and distribution checks](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29888144304)
- [changed-file coverage](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29888144282)

An earlier diagnostic run is not final evidence: its Flatpak artifact succeeded, but manual inspection found that provenance recorded GitHub's synthetic PR merge SHA and that the uploaded checksum path did not match the flattened artifact. Those defects are repaired at this head. The same diagnostic cycle exposed the Snap `@types/node` closure gap and insufficient runtime-copier coverage; both are repaired and regression-tested here.

A later Snap diagnostic built and statically verified the real arm64 package, then exposed that its installed-runtime script changed into a read-only directory while retaining a relative evidence path. This head canonicalizes that evidence directory before the directory change and mutation-tests the invariant. It also removes OIDC and attestation write scopes from every pull-request build step; only the separate non-PR post-build job can attest the checksum-verified uploaded artifact.

Before ready-for-review, the exact-head job artifacts and logs will be manually inspected for architecture, checksums, manifests, SBOM/provenance, installed launcher behavior, lifecycle/uninstall behavior, offline startup, and health output. Any unsupported acceptance item will remain explicitly identified rather than inferred from structural tests.

6b:T24c5,Resolves #16911.

## Problem

The canonical Cloud Android post-build audit passed release `.aab` artifacts straight to `aapt dump badging` / `aapt dump xmltree`. AAPT is APK-only, so every valid App Bundle failed with `no AndroidManifest.xml found` before any strip-policy assertion could run. There was no bundle-aware tooling anywhere in the repo.

## Fix

- **New `packages/app-core/scripts/lib/android-cloud-artifact-audit.mjs`** — bundle-aware audit built on Google's official bundletool **1.18.3**, pinned by SHA-256 (`a099cfa1…8e29`). The pin is enforced at provisioning (`ensureAndroidBundletoolJar`: env-configured JAR must match the digest; otherwise a one-time download into a content-verified cache, atomic rename, no unpinned fallback) and **re-verified immediately before every bundletool spawn** (TOCTOU defense). All bundletool invocations carry a 120 s timeout and surface child-process failures (spawn error, non-zero exit, signal, timeout) as typed `ElizaError`s.
- **`auditAndroidCloudArtifact` now branches on artifact kind** (`resolveAndroidArtifactKind`): APK auditing keeps the exact AAPT badging/xmltree + DEX behavior; AAB auditing runs `bundletool validate`, decodes **every module manifest** (`dump manifest --module=…`, base required, traversal-shaped module names rejected), and scans **every module's `dex/classes*.dex`**. Private LP3 actions, permissions, components, classes, and policy markers remain forbidden in ordinary Cloud/Play/AOSP artifacts — no strip or policy check was weakened; the AOSP/system audit additionally gained the manifest-marker + DEX-marker assertions.
- **Gradle wiring** (`platforms/android/app/build.gradle`): for `elizaCloudBuild` builds, an `auditCloudReleaseAab` finalizer resolves the exact release bundle via the AGP `SingleArtifact.BUNDLE` API and runs the audit-only CLI on it, fail-closed — a failed, skipped, or outcome-less `bundleRelease` is a hard error, never a silent skip. The orchestrator passes `process.execPath` through as `NODE_BINARY` so the finalizer works where `node` is not on Gradle's PATH.
- **New audit-only CLI target**: `node scripts/run-mobile-build.mjs android-cloud-audit [aab-path]`, and the audit emits a machine-readable attestation line (`[mobile-build] android-cloud AAB attestation {json}`) carrying the bundletool version and the artifact SHA-256/size.
- `scripts/lib/eliza-error.mjs` gives the build scripts a local `ElizaError` so they do not depend on `@elizaos/core`'s built dist.

## Tests

`packages/app-core/scripts/run-mobile-build-android-aab-audit.test.mjs` covers the acceptance matrix: valid clean AAB, forbidden manifest marker, forbidden DEX marker (including feature-module-only), malformed/truncated bundle, missing tool, child-process failure/signal/timeout, bundletool digest pinning (configured JAR, download, corrupt download), APK-path regression contract (exact AAPT commands preserved), boundary hardening, and the Gradle wiring contract. An opt-in real lane (`ELIZA_ANDROID_RUN_REAL_AAB_TEST=1`) runs real bundletool against the byte-pinned multi-module fixture from Google's bundletool repo (`test/fixtures/android/install-time-permanent-modules.aab`, Apache-2.0, SHA-256 documented in the fixture README).

## CI follow-up needed (workflow scope)

This branch was prepared with a companion change to `.github/workflows/mobile-build-smoke.yml` (SDK 34→36, 60 min timeout, the real-fixture vitest lane, and a build-and-audit job for the canonical release AAB with attestation verification + evidence upload). The token available on this box has no `workflow` scope, so that file cannot be pushed from here — the delta is attached below for a workflow-scoped account to apply on this branch or as an immediate follow-up.

+          AAB_PATH="packages/app-core/platforms/android/app/build/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab"
