+          const line = fs
+            .readFileSync(logPath, "utf8")
+            .split(/\r?\n/)
+            .filter((candidate) => candidate.startsWith(prefix))
+            .at(-1);

+          }
+          const aabBytes = fs.readFileSync(aabPath);
+          const aabSha256 = crypto.createHash("sha256").update(aabBytes).digest("hex");
+          if (
+            attestation.artifact?.sha256 !== aabSha256 ||
+            attestation.artifact?.sizeBytes !== aabBytes.byteLength
+          ) {
+            throw new Error("release AAB attestation does not match the uploaded artifact");
+          }
+          fs.writeFileSync(outputPath, `${JSON.stringify(attestation, null, 2)}\n`);
+          NODE
+          sha256sum "$AAB_PATH" | tee "$RUNNER_TEMP/android-cloud-release-aab.sha256"
+
+      - name: Upload release AAB audit evidence
+        if: always()
+        uses: actions/upload-artifact@043fb46d1a93c77aae656e7c1c64a875d1fc6a0a
+        with:
+          name: android-cloud-release-aab-audit
+          path: |
+            packages/app-core/platforms/android/app/build/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab
+            ${{ runner.temp }}/android-cloud-release-aab-audit.log
+            ${{ runner.temp }}/android-cloud-release-aab.sha256
+            ${{ runner.temp }}/android-cloud-release-attestation.json
+          retention-days: 7
+          if-no-files-found: warn
```

</details>

6d:T15e6,# Relates to

Relates to #16277.

Dependency #16276 is already merged through #16285. This branch is rebased directly onto that prerequisite at `42838644ed2c871916a1151db145825a1234497d`.

# Outcome

Replaces the legacy single-job release script with an explicit three-stage transaction:

1. A credential-free candidate job verifies the exact repository/ref/SHA, builds and packs the reviewed cohort once, and records immutable plan, cohort, manifest, entrypoint, and tarball integrity.
2. A least-privilege npm job authenticates the planned publisher, binds the normalized registry before mutation, stages missing immutable versions under a cohort-derived candidate tag, verifies the full cohort, promotes the public channel, and removes the staging tags.
3. A finalizer re-reads npm without credentials, binds the Git push destination, pushes only the exact planned annotated tag, and creates or verifies a GitHub Release whose repository, tag, target commit, and prerelease identity match the plan.

Every mutation command requires the complete release identity plus the canonical plan digest. Exclusive state locks and monotonic `registry-bound` / `git-bound` transitions make interruption retries idempotent without permitting a target, publisher, candidate-tag, ref, or artifact swap.

# Pack-once invariant

The obsolete `verify-npm-pack-dist.mjs` helper is removed because its only caller was the legacy workflow and it rebuilt plus dry-packed packages a second time. Validation is not reduced: candidate construction now performs the single real `npm pack`, checks the actual `.tgz` bytes, verifies every declared `main` / `module` / `types` / `bin` / concrete `exports` target is present, records SHA-512 hex and SRI, and revalidates the same immutable tarballs before every side effect. Removing the legacy helper is what preserves the issue's build/test/pack-once contract and clears the script-orphan audit.

# Risk and rollout

Risk is high because this is public release infrastructure. The workflow remains manual/reusable, uses one global non-cancelling concurrency group, reserves failed beta versions, separates trusted tooling from candidate source, and never pushes a branch. This PR did not contact public npm for publication or create/update any public Git tag or GitHub Release.

6e:T2b68,Closes #16896.

## Why

This replaces the unsafe timeout/fallback proposed in #16882 and the mocked timing gate in #16820.

A generic `Promise.race` around prompt providers is not latency control. It returns apparently valid empty context, leaves the losing operation running, can cache the fabricated fallback, and hides the component that is actually broken. Reducing that race from 30 seconds to 3 seconds only makes the hidden failure happen sooner.

The runtime should execute independent work concurrently, reuse valid work maximally, cancel work only through a real `AbortSignal`, and report every stage precisely enough that a slow request names its cause.

## Runtime behavior

- Runs every selected prompt provider concurrently with `Promise.all`.
- Reuses all valid per-turn, stable, and max-cache provider results unless the caller explicitly refreshes a provider.
- Coalesces identical in-flight provider work for the same message/provider.
- Makes the planner's second composition max-cache and refreshes only `RECENT_MESSAGES` when the early reply changed it.
- Passes the owning request/stream `AbortSignal` through provider, document-recall, embedding, OpenAI, and ElizaCloud embedding paths.
- Throws and reports provider failures instead of manufacturing empty prompt context.
- Moves semantic document recall into the parallel `DOCUMENTS` provider; the serial pre-model augmentation is lexical only and warms clean-prompt embedding out of band.
- Makes available-app registry lookup stale-while-revalidate and prewarmed, and makes wallet prompt composition cache-only with a coalesced background refresh.
- Removes abandoned timeout races and fabricated `[]`/`null` results from orchestrator provider/database paths.
- Isolates model-usage capture per async request so concurrent turns on one runtime cannot contaminate each other's call or token totals.

## Full request telemetry

One `chat-request` flow now measures the complete path from request receipt through response finalization, with exclusive stage accounting for:

`document-augmentation`, `providers`, `embedding`, `model-queue`, `model-routing`, `model-preprocess`, `llm-inference`, `model-postprocess`, `actions`, `evaluators`, `planner-overhead`, `response-finalization`, `message-service-overhead`, and `unattributed`.

It also records first model token, first visible output, reply delivery, finalization, provider outcome/cache/coalescing, model provider/attempt/outcome, prompt/completion/total tokens, and prompt-cache read/creation tokens when the model SDK reports them. Summaries, histograms, provider outcomes, and flows are available from the development latency endpoint.

## Timeout policy

Removed:

- 30-second/3-second prompt-provider `Promise.race` fallback from #16882.
- 4-second document lookup race that did not cancel the lookup.
- 5-second recovery LLM call that hid a broken primary path.
- 2-second orchestrator provider/database races that returned fake empty values.

Retained only real cancellation or non-critical lifecycle boundaries:

- outer chat request: 180 seconds, aborts the owned request tree;
- ElizaCloud text generation: 120 seconds, aborts the HTTP request;
- embeddings: 60 seconds, aborts the HTTP request/backoff and cannot fall through after abort;
- wallet RPC: 4 seconds through viem transport cancellation;
- optional title generation: 5 seconds, outside reply correctness;
- post-delivery side effect: 30 seconds, detached and diagnostically reported.

These retained boundaries do not turn a failure into healthy-empty prompt state. A 30-second interactive response is still broken; telemetry now identifies the stage, while the outer boundary prevents leaked work.

## Exact provider telemetry

- cache coverage: **24/24 providers on every one of 30 samples**
- effective parallelism: min **6.62x**, p50 **7.75x**, p95 **8.45x**, max **8.56x**, mean **7.84x**

**95–97% of measured end-to-end latency was upstream OpenAI inference.** Prompt providers, document augmentation, evaluators, and local orchestration were tens of milliseconds combined. No request approached 30 seconds and there was no synthetic timeout fallback.

The live scenario intentionally uses the scenario runner's zero-vector embedding fallback, so its sub-millisecond embedding span is not presented as embedding performance. A separate real local GTE-small fp16 GGUF benchmark on an Apple M4 Max measured **322 ms cold model load**, **3.96 ms median single-text embedding**, and batch throughput of **321/s at batch 1**, **1114/s at batch 4**, **1593/s at batch 8**, and **2516/s at batch 16**.

OpenAI did not report a positive prompt-cache token count in this sample. Provider-context cache behavior is independently measured above at 24/24 hits; the response DTO now preserves model prompt-cache fields whenever the SDK reports them.

## #16820 review and replacement

#16820 is a deterministic browser-test concept, not production latency telemetry: it mocks `fetch` and SSE, starts timing before the click, treats a DOM mutation as a paint, inserts a synthetic 35-second idle, and computes p95 from two samples.

The replacement Chromium test drives the real chat UI, starts at the click, observes React commits after `requestAnimationFrame`, requires at least three distinct assistant text lengths before completion, and proves incremental rendering rather than one final paint. It remains a deterministic client-render regression test and is not mislabeled as server/model latency.

6f:T13ad,Closes #16960.
Closes #16975.

Follow-up to #16925. The response-tail change in that PR was real, but ordinary Worker chat requests could still hit Postgres before model dispatch for authentication, billing admission, agent resolution, conversation history, and voice scope checks.

## What changed

- Makes the ordinary user/organization Worker inference path cache-only before model dispatch.
- Treats cold cache entries as a short `503` warming state and schedules authoritative hydration with `waitUntil`; stale entries are served immediately and refreshed asynchronously.
- Adds a per-agent/per-room `SharedRuntimeConversation` Durable Object. Durable Object storage is the ordered, authoritative live conversation state; legacy history migration and Postgres mirrors run off the response path.
- Defers shared-tier billing admission/accounting and linked-character hydration without allowing an uncached database fallback into the LLM hot path.
- Applies the same cache-only contract to REST chat, SSE chat, OpenAI-compatible chat completions, Anthropic-compatible messages, the agent bridge, and voice scope resolution.
- Preserves synchronous reservation-coupled semantics for explicit affiliate/app-monetization and non-Worker fallback paths.
- Registers sliding cache refreshes with the Worker execution context so refresh work is observable and allowed to finish.

## CI coverage policy

- Removes the 50% per-file changed-source coverage floor.
- Removes the shared Vitest 25/25/25/15 global percentage floors.
- Removes app-core surface percentage thresholds and threshold-only reporting.
- Removes zero-valued CLI threshold overrides and stale threshold recommendations.
- Keeps the useful non-percentage guarantees: changed executable source must appear in LCOV, it cannot have zero executable lines, and changed production code still requires changed tests.
- Correctly excludes `.d.ts`, `.d.mts`, and `.d.cts` declarations from changed executable-source classification.

70:T2914,## Summary

- run the scheduled iOS Local lane through the canonical composition: fresh simulator app install, OS-delivered auth callback, then the strict full-Bun local-model probe
- write/read Capacitor preferences inside the simulator domain, repair the Xcode 16.4 simulator Swift-library lookup for `simctl` children, and pre-approve the app's URL scheme without replacing unrelated LaunchServices entries
- share one strict app-core full-Bun probe with the renderer, preserve the exact model input/output receipt, and render an explicit terminal pass/fail state only after the terminal receipt is persisted
- split platform entrypoint seams into source modules with honest source-path tests; changed-source coverage passes without a classifier or policy exemption
- hard-gate the redacted scheme/auth/model receipts, exact head and model hashes, unified logs, JPG, and H.264 MP4

Addresses #13577. Scoped contribution to #13562.

## Root cause and repair

The hosted lane exposed three independent simulator boundaries in sequence:

1. A host-path `defaults write` could exit successfully without producing simulator-readable Capacitor Preferences. The lane now executes `xcrun simctl spawn <udid> defaults ...`, supports Capacitor's prefixed key plus its raw alias, and requires a native readback before launch.
2. Xcode 16.4 with the iOS 18.5 simulator could launch-abort while resolving `/usr/lib/swift/libswiftWebKit.dylib`. The workflow supplies Apple's simulator-runtime Cryptex fallback only to `simctl` child processes; it does not change the production deployment target.
3. A fresh simulator presented an untappable `Open in “Eliza”?` sheet for `simctl openurl`, leaving auth at `phase=requested`. The orchestrator now preserves the existing LaunchServices dictionary, writes and reads back `com.apple.CoreSimulator.CoreSimulatorBridge-->elizaos = ai.elizaos.app`, emits a redacted receipt, and reboots once when that mapping changes.

Mutation-sensitive contracts prove unapproved=`confirmation-blocked`, approved=`deliver-to-app`, wrong-bundle repair, idempotence, simulator-native preference readback, and the complete strict non-streaming + streaming model contract.

71:T321f,Closes #17098

## Outcome

This makes chat streaming a single, observable provider → runtime → SSE → React → database pipeline and removes latency policy from correctness CI.

The runtime now has one owner for assistant-message persistence, the SSE completion frame carries the exact committed IDs, the client reconciles optimistic messages in place, and normal streaming performs no post-response history reload. Failed persistence emits an error and never a fake `done`.

## Runtime and persistence

- Return the exact response-memory IDs committed by `DefaultMessageService`.
- Reuse those IDs in the chat route instead of writing or reading the assistant response a second time.
- Preserve callback-before-persistence delivery for fast first response, with the same-room persistence barrier added upstream, while withholding SSE `done` until the write succeeds.
- Mark genuinely ephemeral responses explicitly; never invent a database ID.
- Treat delta streams as deltas. The previous overlap heuristic could corrupt `Fast streaming stays smooth.` into `Fastreaming staysmooth.`.
- Remove the hidden 30-second runtime-ready gate.
- Remove the implicit 180-second chat-generation abort, 60-second browser SSE idle abort, 60-second trajectory-finalization abort, and 5-second conversation-restore race. Cancellation is now caller-owned through `AbortSignal` unless a caller explicitly configures a boundary.
- Move trajectory persistence/finalization off the visible-response path while tracking it as post-delivery work.

## Providers, cache, and database

- Providers selected for a compose run execute concurrently through one `Promise.all`.
- Stage 1 excludes context it cannot consume; the planner reuses all already-composed provider results and refreshes only `RECENT_MESSAGES` when an early reply changed history.
- Replace mutable one-second identity/entity caches with exact turn-scoped promise memoization, rejection eviction, and explicit invalidation.
- Memoize sender-role, room, and related-entity reads within a turn.
- Skip attachment-history database work for ordinary text turns.
- Make SQL memory creation one atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING`, removing the read-before-write race and extra database round trip.
- Cache only successful shared-agent scope authorization. Failed 401/403/404 hydration is never persisted, so a corrected credential, tier, or agent row is observed on the next authoritative attempt instead of serving a stale denial for 30 seconds.

## Browser behavior

- Reconcile optimistic user/assistant IDs in place from the completion frame.
- Do not reload conversation history after an ordinary successful response.
- Reload only when the response is action/incomplete or committed IDs are unavailable.
- Replace requestAnimationFrame token throttling—which can stop making progress in background tabs—with microtask burst coalescing and a synchronous terminal flush.
- Remove the reconnect debounce/speculative resend path; user-triggered resend starts immediately and remains explicit.
- Add the missing `rate_limited` SSE contract.

## CI and telemetry

- Replace the ~1,000-line latency workflow with a small, manual, observational workflow.
- Remove numeric latency acceptance thresholds, regression ratchets, production-code-changed/test-changed gates, and the workflow self-test enforcing those policies.
- Run direct and gateway observations concurrently and upload raw JSONL. Telemetry reports numbers; it does not decide correctness.
- Remove numeric enforcement from inference-auth latency tooling.
- Add a real browser telemetry harness using the production route, production stream client, `useChatSend`, React transcript, real HTTP/SSE, and PGLite.
- Expand the Cerebras runner to cover the complete production chat flow plus an explicit concurrent sweep of every registered provider.

## PR review

- #16882: closed unmerged. The proposed provider deadline would turn latency into missing context and make a slow dependency look healthy.
- #16820: closed unmerged. Browser telemetry is retained here, but the numeric merge gate and changed-production-requires-changed-test policy are removed.
- #17037: merged and retained. Dashboard replies have one transport owner.
- #17057: merged; its useful DB coalescing is retained, while this branch replaces mutable TTL-based identity/entity reuse with exact turn-scoped memoization.
- #17060: merged and retained. Cerebras strict object schemas remain recursively closed instead of being stripped.
- #17076: merged and retained as a cache-only shared inference hot path. This branch removes its remaining negative-scope TTL, which could serve stale denial after state was already corrected.

- 60/60 turns succeeded and each made exactly one live LLM call.
- 1,440/1,440 provider executions completed with zero errors.
- Cerebras reported 92,416 cached input tokens out of 150,504 prompt tokens
  (61.4043%).
- Every distinct proof token was present in both the returned and streamed
  response.

The `50 ms` figure is not normal synchronous message-service overhead. Across
both runs the exclusive wrapper remainder is 4.0 ms at p50. The outlier run
contained one synchronized PGLite/storage stall: participant-state reached
91 ms, FACTS 55 ms, RECENT_MESSAGES 50 ms, and delivery persistence 57 ms in
the same turn. Another turn inflated many otherwise trivial providers together.
That shape proves process-wide database/event-loop scheduling, not serial
provider execution. The 149.989 ms wrapper outlier was a gap between compose
completion and stage-one preprocessing; it disappeared on the immediate repeat
(22 ms max), consistent with Bun GC/event-loop scheduling.

The remaining end-to-end outliers were model-side: the repeat had two Cerebras
calls at 2.1–2.56 seconds while local provider and delivery spans remained
normal. Detached message events, evaluators, and run-ended work occur after
terminal response visibility; they can still contend with local PGLite, but no
longer delay the visible response.

73:T38e0,# fix(app): lifecycle-safe renderer services + self-starting LifeOps activity capture

Closes #16504

## What & why

The LifeOps activity-signal capture used to hang off a React effect (`LifeOpsActivitySignalsEffect` mounted in `main.tsx`), so capture lived and died with one component render, and the proposed replacement (`appRegister` side-effect entry) was unsafe to merge: the app's deferred-import cache was keyed by bare package name (a register entry and the root facade would share one promise), side-effect loaders ran in every renderer window, and returned disposers were discarded. This PR builds the prerequisites the issue specifies, in order, then moves the capture onto them.

### 1. Role-qualified loader identities (`packages/app`)
- `vite/app-side-effect-modules.ts` now emits `key: "<packageName>#<mode>"` plus explicit `packageName`/`mode` fields; the shared dynamic-import cache (`src/app-module-cache.ts`, extracted from `main.tsx`) is keyed by that role-qualified identity, so a `/register` entry can never share (or suppress) the cached promise of the package-root facade.
- Production-loader proof: `src/plugin-registrations.test.ts` runs the **real Vite transform** over `plugin-registrations.ts` and asserts Personal Assistant's injected loader imports `src/register.ts` (never `ui.ts`/`index.ts`) under `"@elizaos/plugin-personal-assistant#register"`, that no generated key collides with any `cachedDynamicImport` key in `main.tsx`, and that facade+register loaders requested together each run exactly once with the correct namespaces. `register.loader-contract.test.ts` (in the plugin) loads the REAL `ui.ts` and `register.ts` modules through the real cache under the production identities.

### 2. Renderer-service lifecycle registry (`packages/ui/src/platform/renderer-services.ts`)
- `registerRendererService({ id, shells, start })` + `startRendererServiceHost({ shell })`: plugins declare scoped services instead of starting work at import time; the host starts only shell-eligible definitions, **retains every returned cleanup**, and tears down on `pagehide`, host replacement, and same-id re-registration (HMR).
- Race-safe: stopping during an awaited `start` aborts the instance's `AbortSignal` and runs the late-resolved cleanup immediately; a `start` that resolves without a cleanup function is reported as a contract violation (discarded disposers are the bug this exists to prevent). Failures are observable (`failed` state + error reporter), never healthy-looking absence.
- Store lives on `globalThis` under `Symbol.for(...)` so duplicated module evaluation (HMR/mixed chunks) cannot fork ownership of running instances.

### 3. Explicit renderer scope (`packages/app/src/renderer-shell-scope.ts`)
- Pure classification of the boot inputs `main.tsx` already resolves: `main | popout | detached | chat-overlay | tray-popover | phone-companion | app-window | model-tester | embed`. The shell installs the host with this scope before any side-effect registration module loads (both on the normal boot and the model-tester early-return path).

### 4. LifeOps capture as an imperative controller (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant`)
- `src/lifeops/activity-signals-capture.ts` replaces `src/hooks/useLifeOpsActivitySignals.ts` (React hook + app-shell mount + stale module declarations deleted). Registered by `src/register.ts` as `shells: ["main"]` — never popouts, detached shells, phone companion, app windows, model tester, or embeds.
- **Idempotent**: second start returns the active stop; stop fully releases the singleton for clean re-init.
- **Race-safe stop**: every awaited native step (permission check, `addListener`, `startMonitoring`, background-refresh scheduling) re-checks the stop flag; stopping mid-start removes the late listener handle, stands the engaged monitor down, and never installs a late poller interval.
- **Consent gate**: native monitoring starts only when permission status is already `granted`/`not-applicable`; this background service never prompts (the settings UI owns requesting); a denial surfaces as a `permission_unavailable` status event and is re-checked on app resume so a grant made in OS Settings activates without restart.
- **Expected vs unexpected failures**: runtime-not-ready (503 on the capture path) and transport/network/timeout errors stand the capture down quietly until the ready-poll recovers; anything else emits a `capture_error` status event + prefixed `console.error` instead of collapsing into "runtime not ready".
- Manifest marker added last (`elizaos.appRegister: "register"` + `sideEffects`), after the loader contract was fixed and proven.

## Tests (all real-path; the registry under test is the real one)

- `packages/ui/src/platform/renderer-services.test.ts` — 16 tests: ordering both ways, shell scoping, disposer retention, pagehide, host replacement, HMR re-registration, throwing cleanup isolation, stop-during-awaited-start (late cleanup runs), rejected-after-stop, no-cleanup contract violation, failed-service isolation.
- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/register.test.ts` — drives the REAL registry: registers at import without starting, never starts in popout/detached, starts+stops under a main host, re-initializes on host replacement, tears down on pagehide.
- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/activity-signals-capture.test.ts` — 21 tests incl. idempotency, stop→start re-init, consent gate (granted / denied / prompt-never / resume pickup), stop racing `addListener` and `startMonitoring`, concurrent-trigger single start, capture_error surfacing, transport-vs-unexpected status-probe split, full listener/interval teardown. Mocks are confined to the process boundaries (native Capacitor bridge + HTTP client), not the controller under test.
- `packages/app/src/renderer-shell-scope.test.ts`, `src/plugin-registrations.test.ts` (real filesystem scan + real Vite transform), entrypoint gate tests updated for the host install.

# Risks

**Medium.** The installer intentionally mutates host package/browser state when
invoked. Risk is bounded by direct executable argv (never a shell), explicit
package-manager detection, noninteractive flags, `sudo -n`, an inspectable
`--dry-run`, and post-install behavioral probes. It does not change shell
profiles, developer configuration, GitHub authentication, repository
permissions, or credentials.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds one shared requirement catalog for OCR, ffmpeg/ffprobe, Playwright
  Chromium, and the optional GitHub CLI capability.
- Makes `bun run evidence:install-tools` install or repair the required
  toolchain through Homebrew, apt/dnf/yum/apk/pacman/zypper, or WinGet.
- Replaces presence checks with real OCR recognition, media
  encode/probe/decode, and headless Chromium launch probes.
- Adds safe dry-run, strict and normalized JSON output, idempotent reruns,
  Windows process-local PATH refresh, focused contract tests, operator
  documentation, and a read-only Ubuntu/macOS/Windows smoke workflow.
- Keeps GitHub support deliberately narrow: `--github` installs/verifies the
  optional `gh` executable only. It never checks or modifies auth, tokens, API
  access, or repository permissions; upload/auth behavior remains in #16991.

## What kind of change is this?

Infrastructure/build improvement and compatibility fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation is required and has been updated in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, including
the canonical command, flags, supported package managers, privilege/network
expectations, optional capability semantics, Windows PATH behavior, and strict
doctor usage.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/develop/CONTRIBUTING.md).

# What does this PR do?

Live-verifies the two residual MVP workstream-1 catalog legs and fixes every product defect those live runs exposed:

- **Leg 1 (L1, child/student deadlines):** all 6 scenarios (`child-book-report-backplan`, `child-book-report-cancel-reask`, `child-morning-routine-plain-words`, `student-term-paper-night-owl-deadline`, `student-lab-report-distractor-deadline`, `student-report-two-phase-commit`) verified live and flipped to `verified` in `_catalogs/child-student-deadlines.catalog.json` — 6/6.
- **Leg 2 (FR1, first-run onboarding):** new `_catalogs/first-run-onboarding.catalog.json` wired into the coverage gate (`packages/scripts/check-lifeops-persona-catalog-coverage.mjs`, strict-evidence pack), all 4 scenarios verified live — 4/4.
- **Product fixes from the live runs** (each root-caused from a hand-read failing trajectory, each with unit tests):
  - consent gate, reminder routing, dup guard, CLI reply hygiene (first sweep);
  - deterministic retraction of un-previewed saves (one-shot undo handle) + persisted lead-up nudges;
  - sentence-scoped consent (a leading "yes" no longer vetoed by a later negation), deadline-aware slot extraction, confirm-with-additions classification;
  - milestone reminder plans (multi-phase asks store real steps), wake-anchored customize pack, honest connector status (no `connected:true` for a bare registration), fabricated setup-claim vocabulary;
  - lead-up nudges expressed in the store's contract (cadence `visibilityLeadMinutes` + non-negative step offsets — negative offsets were rejected by the service and collapsed every lead-up plan to a single due-time step);
  - **firstRun provider never composed into planner state** (final commit, see below).

## The fast-start defect and fix

The final sweep left exactly one failing row: `first-run-fast-start-seeds-first-reminder` (judge **0.00**). Hand-reading the trajectory showed the turn-1 planner prompt contained **no first-run line at all** — `firstRunProvider` is `dynamic: true` with no `contexts`/`contextGate`/`alwaysInResponseState`, `composeState`'s default path skips dynamic providers, and `selectV5PlannerStateProviderNames` only adds always-on or context-gated names. The affordance was invisible on every planner turn, so the model answered a fresh-boot "set me up with sensible defaults" ask with:

> "Welcome aboard! Good news — there's nothing you have to configure to get going. I'm already running on sensible defaults … Otherwise, you're all set — what would you like to do first?"

Judge: *"It denies a needed setup flow and prematurely claims setup is complete without accepting defaults or asking questions."* (The defaults pack still seeded from the deterministic wake-time path — the damage was the dishonest turn-1 reply.)

Fix (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/providers/first-run.ts`): the provider now opts into `alwaysInResponseState: true` with `roleGate: { minRole: "OWNER" }` and `settings`/`general`/`system` contexts — the exact `pendingApprovals` precedent. It already self-silences once first-run completes. Declaration pinned in `test/first-run-provider.test.ts`; the always-on mechanism itself is pinned in core (`services/message.planner-provider-selection.test.ts`).

Re-run after the fix (same isolated live harness): **judge 1.0**, turn 1 engages the flow honestly:

> "Welcome aboard! Quick heads up — first-run setup isn't done yet, so let's knock it out fast. I just need three things and I'll take the sensible defaults on everything else: 1. What should I call you (and want a wake time for morning check-ins)? …"

and the store shows the full defaults pack with the gm cron anchored to the answered wake time (`30 6 * * *` for "i usually wake up around 6:30am").

# Evidence

**On the model choice:** the issue names Cerebras gpt-oss-120b as the *precedent* actor model; no Cerebras credential was available to this lane, so the actor is **claude-opus-4-7 live via the CLI subscription** — the repo's other precedented live path (`ELIZA_CHAT_VIA_CLI=claude`, catalog rows verified this way on 2026-07-06). The actor is a real live LLM driving the real chat conductor, and the judge is a separate independent model (`gpt-5.6-sol`, `SCENARIO_JUDGE_REQUIRE_INDEPENDENT=1`); every row records the actual model used.

Harness: one isolated runner process per scenario — `ELIZA_CHAT_VIA_CLI=claude` (claude-opus-4-7 via CLI subscription, `ELIZA_PLANNER_NATIVE_TOOLS=0`), independent judge `gpt-5.6-sol` via a local OpenAI-compatible shim (`SCENARIO_JUDGE_REQUIRE_INDEPENDENT=1`), `TZ=UTC`, per-run PGLite preserved and queried afterward. output   lifeops:first-run:default:local-backup  {"kind":"cron","expression":"0 */6 * * *","tz":"UTC"}
```

</details>

Frontend evidence rows: **N/A — no UI surface changed**; this branch is runtime/provider/action logic, scenario harness, and catalog ledgers. All proof is live-LLM trajectories, structured runner logs, and persisted store rows (the domain artifacts for these scenarios).

## Exact coverage-gate output (maintainer acceptance ask)

```
$ node packages/scripts/check-lifeops-persona-catalog-coverage.mjs --pack L1 --require-verified
LifeOps persona scenario catalog coverage
L1 6 authored (target 6), 6/6 verified (child-student-deadlines.catalog.json)
Total: 6 authored (target 6), 6/6 verified, 0 unverified
exit=0

$ node packages/scripts/check-lifeops-persona-catalog-coverage.mjs --pack FR1 --require-verified
LifeOps persona scenario catalog coverage
FR1 4 authored (target 4), 4/4 verified (first-run-onboarding.catalog.json)
Total: 4 authored (target 4), 4/4 verified, 0 unverified
exit=0
```

`first-run-onboarding.catalog.json` is part of the checked catalog inventory (`EXPECTED_CATALOGS` in `check-lifeops-persona-catalog-coverage.mjs`) and both L1 and FR1 are **strict-evidence packs**: the gate rejects any verified row missing a structured evidence object (model, judge, judgeScore, pointer, artifactReceipt) — pinned by `packages/scripts/__tests__/lifeops-catalog-coverage.test.ts`, including a tamper test that deletes a row's evidence and asserts exit 1.

**Conductor path, not direct service calls:** every first-run run above drives the real chat conductor — each turn is an inbound message through the message service with recorded `messageHandler` and `planner` trajectory stages (see the per-scenario report excerpts). The seeded pack rows come out of the production scheduled-task store afterward; no scenario calls `FirstRunService` directly.

**Negative store artifacts (as demanded):** cancel-reask's store contains no canceled science-report reminder (only the two mock seeds + the confirmed Ms.-Rivera replacement); lab-report's store contains no pizza task.

# Residual (documented, not silently dropped)

The pre-fix fast-start run also exposed a **core guard bypass**: when the planner emits a terminal `REPLY` tool call, `planner-loop.ts` ships `params.text` directly as the final message — the `REPLY` action handler (which carries the `replyClaimsCompletedSideEffect` fabricated-claim guard, `packages/core/src/features/basic-capabilities/actions/reply.ts:415`) never runs, and the Stage-1 evaluator twin does not see planner-path replies. That is how "you're all set" shipped even though the claim vocabulary matches the guard. With the provider fix the model no longer fabricates here, but the bypass remains reachable for other fabricated-completion shapes. Deliberately **not** patched in this PR: a planner-loop claim check needs its own false-positive analysis (claims legitimately grounded in *prior-turn* actions) and its own verification sweep. Flagged in the issue comment for a dedicated lane.

76:Tdc1,## Summary

Proposes one implementation-ready coordination contract for Sol, Nubs' agent, Shaw's agent, and future team agents.

This is additive and does not replace or edit `.github/FLEET.md`. It includes:

- canonical work, team, agent, and session identity
- TTL leases with renewal, fencing generations, and zombie reclamation
- pillar ownership plus exactly one accountable owner per failure class
- typed dependency and collision graph with deterministic dispatch
- two-phase handoffs and immutable evidence/review receipts
- independent review, merge authority, and HITL rules
- strict Smithers vs Merge Steward authority boundary
- GitHub-now and Forgejo-later adapters with a single write-authority epoch
- JSON Schema and network-free deterministic conformance tests

## Why

The proposal is grounded in observed collisions: the parallel FLEET/AGENT_COORDINATION governance split, concurrent Seeker builds, stale prod-deploy takeover, branch rescue overlap, lane-tag identity confusion, board/discussion/PR drift, and potential Smithers/Merge Steward split-brain.

- document workspace editor selection, compiler API usage, declaration policy, and rollback

## Reproduction

Stable TypeScript does not ship the retired preview binary:

```text

79:T1b16,## Summary

Closes #16441. One of five cards replacing the closed monolith #16200 — this one
delivers the **unified structural intent vocabulary + routing authority** for
chat, voice, and transcription launches, independently rebuilt on `develop`
(nothing cherry-picked from #16200).

Today every launch surface is ad-hoc: iOS App Intents / Siri, Android app-actions
+ shortcuts, desktop deep links, tray/widget controls, and notification taps each
emit an `elizaos://…?action=<free-form string>&text=…&voice=1` deep link, and the
only consumer (`assistant-launch-payload.ts`) handles **chat send only**, keyed on
a free-form `action` string with a plain in-memory `Set` for dedupe. There is no
typed vocabulary, no voice/transcription launch path, no prerequisite handling,
and no dedupe across windows / restored sessions.

This PR adds `packages/ui/src/os-intent/` — the one place a launch is expressed as
a typed `OsIntent` and routed by **structural fields only** to the single shared
shell controller.

### Intent vocabulary (`contract.ts`)

Discriminated-union intents, each with a stable `intentId` (idempotency key),
`source`, optional `issuedAt`, declared prerequisites, target, and typed outcomes:

| Intent | Target | Auto-start | Prerequisites |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `open-chat` | chat | no | session |
| `send` | chat | no | session |
| `start-voice` (`converse`/`dictate`) | voice | **yes** | session, unlocked, foreground, microphone, voice-capture |
| `stop-voice` | voice | no | — (always allowed) |
| `start-transcription` | transcription | **yes** | unlocked, foreground, microphone, voice-capture |
| `stop-transcription` | transcription | no | — (always allowed) |
| `continue-conversation` | chat | no | session |

### Routing authority (`router.ts` + `dedupe.ts`)

`routeIntent(intent, context, dedupeStore)` returns a typed `IntentOutcome`
(`routed` / `duplicate` / `stale` / `blocked` / `consent-required` / `degraded`)
and, when routed, the `IntentControllerCommand[]` to apply. Evaluation order:

1. **Stale** — `issuedAt` older than `maxIntentAgeMs` → `stale` (never fires a
   launch the user has moved on from).
2. **Duplicate** — `intentId` already applied → `duplicate`. Only *routed* intents
   are recorded, so a `blocked` intent retried after the user grants permission
   still routes.
3. **Degraded** — device cannot honor it (voice unsupported / sandboxed) →
   `degraded` (visible unavailable state, never a silent no-op).
4. **Blocked** — recoverable prerequisite unmet (unauthenticated / auth-expired /
   locked / backgrounded / microphone-denied) → `blocked` with the missing
   prerequisites.
5. **Consent** — an auto-start (mic) intent without recorded consent →
   `consent-required` (explicit, reversible, consent-aware).
6. **Routed** — emit commands + record `intentId`.

Routing is **structural only** — it switches on `intent.type` and reads the
context; it never inspects prompt or transcript text. The free-form native
`action` string is mapped to a typed intent once, at the decode boundary.

**Idempotent dedupe across every redelivery path** — deep-link retries, re-tapped
notifications, multiple windows, and restored/crashed-then-reopened sessions all
carry the same `intentId`. `IntentDedupeStore` is a pure, clock-injected,
TTL-pruned store with `snapshot()` / seeding so a restored session rehydrates the
already-applied set and does not re-fire.

### Boundary decoder (`decode.ts`)

`decodeOsIntent(raw)` and `decodeDeepLinkIntent(url)` validate untrusted intent
payloads (deep links, notifications, native bridges) into a typed `OsIntent` or an
explicit typed failure (`{ ok: false, error }`) — never a fabricated-valid
default, never a throw on a single bad frame (error-policy J3). Matches the typed
transcript-event decoder style landed by sibling #16440.

### One engine (`apply-command.ts`)

`applyOsIntentCommand(controller, command)` runs a routed command against the one
live `ShellController` — the same engine the shared cross-window controller
(#16442) owns. The `IntentControllerCommand` union is the subset of #16442's
`ShellControllerCommand` that intents produce, kept structurally identical so no
intent ever spins up a second chat/voice session. Adapters
(`fromAssistantLaunchPayload`, `fromDeepLinkUrl`) let the existing entry points
conform.

## Test evidence

Comprehensive per-file vitest (jsdom/node, deterministic) covering the full case
matrix from the card: invalid/stale intents, locked device, missing permissions,
auth expiry, concurrency (interleaved routing sharing one dedupe store),
background/foreground, crash recovery (snapshot → rehydrate → no re-fire), and
duplicate-start prevention across deep-links / notifications / windows / retries /
restored sessions. Run: `bun run --cwd packages/ui test os-intent`.

## Scope / what is device-gated

- **Web/desktop-verifiable (this PR):** the vocabulary, decoder, dedupe store,
  routing authority, and command executor — all covered by fast vitest.
- **Device-gated (NOT verified here — no device pass run):** wiring the vocabulary
  into the live app-shell launch consumer and the per-platform native invocation
  (iOS App Intents / Siri, Android app-actions + Quick Settings tiles, desktop
  deep-link/tray/hotkey) requires real iOS-sim / Android-emu / packaged-desktop
  passes. The vocabulary + adapters are defined so those surfaces can conform;
  the deep-link `action` strings the native spine already emits (`ask`, `chat`,
  `voice`, `smart-reply`) map onto typed intents in `decode.ts`. I did **not** run
  device verification and do not claim it.

## Evidence

Non-visual TypeScript library module (`packages/ui/src/os-intent/*.ts`) + vitest.
No React component, view, route, or agent/model surface is touched.

<!-- evidence-row:after-screenshots -->

7a:T1f1a,## Summary

- add an authenticated native Cloud pairing exchange for Capacitor/native recovery without weakening the public browser route
- atomically bind one-time token consumption and credential retrieval to user, organization, agent, dedicated-agent origin, expiry, and a row-locked sandbox ownership snapshot
- keep managed Cloud recovery on Cloud-specific reauthenticate/retry/manage surfaces instead of the self-hosted owner-password wall
- make Cloud reauthentication explicitly purge only the stale canonical Cloud credential and rearm recovery only after a confirmed authenticated snapshot
- add route, repository/service integration, recovery-state, UI-gate, and Windows quarantine-runner regression coverage

## Root cause

Native recovery reused the public browser `POST /api/auth/pair` contract. That route intentionally requires a browser `Origin`, while Capacitor native fetch does not synthesize one, so the exchange failed with 400. Recovery then fell through to the generic self-hosted `LoginView`, which is invalid for a managed Cloud target.

## Review correction

The draft was not fully atomic: it read sandbox ownership and `ELIZA_API_TOKEN` before consuming the token, leaving a transfer/rotation TOCTOU window. The corrected repository claim locks the token row and sandbox row in one observed transaction, validates the credential before burning the token, and rechecks expiry at claim time after lock acquisition. Route code now only translates the typed claim result.

## Security contract

The browser endpoint remains public and exact-Origin-bound. The new native endpoint requires a nonempty `Authorization: Bearer` credential resolved by `requireAuthOrApiKeyWithOrg`: that bearer may be either a Steward session JWT (`authMethod: "session"`) or an `eliza_*` Cloud API key presented as the bearer value (`authMethod: "api_key"`) — the API-key form is deliberate and test-blessed ("accepts the bearer API key returned by native device-code login" in `route.test.ts`), because native device-code login yields an API key rather than a session JWT. Competing credential headers (`X-API-Key`, wallet signature headers) are rejected outright so exactly one auth path is in play. The route then validates the token/agent/origin tuple, checks user and organization ownership, and consumes the token atomically only while the matching sandbox remains owned by that organization. Invalid or missing sandbox API credentials do not burn the one-time token.

7b:T1870,## What

Implements the core of #16440: **one typed, append-only transcript-event contract
(`eliza.native-transcript/v1`)** meant to be rendered identically by the iOS,
Android, desktop, and web shells, plus the web/DOM renderer, a language-neutral
golden fixture, and a full conformance test matrix.

Rebuilt fresh from `develop`. Does **not** cherry-pick the closed monolith #16200,
and deliberately does **not** reintroduce its banned anti-pattern of bucketing
state by `text.length` — every reduction/render decision here is made from
**structural fields only** (the `type` discriminant, `phase`/`status`/`final`
flags, stable ids, and the monotonic `seq`).

## Contract (`packages/ui/src/native-transcript/`)

- `contract.ts` — discriminated-union `TranscriptEvent` (8 types: `stt.partial`,
  `stt.final`, `agent.text`, `tool.state`, `tts.audio`, `cancel`, `error`,
  `reconnect`) + the reduced `TranscriptViewModel` (ordered `TranscriptItem[]` +
  transient `speaking`/`connection`). Each event is a self-contained **snapshot**
  (not a delta), so a bridge that dupes/reorders/drops a frame never buffers.
- `decode.ts` — strict boundary decoder. Every field validated; a malformed frame
  yields a typed `{ ok: false, error }` (never a fabricated default, never a throw
  on per-event input) — error-policy **J3**. `decodeTranscriptStream` keeps good
  events and collects rejected ones with index + reason.
- `reduce.ts` — the pure reducer that IS the behavioral spec: **dedupe** (unique
  `seq` → repeat is a no-op), **stable ordering** (item renders at its first-seen
  `seq`), **late-event** guard (per-item `revSeq`; a late partial after a final is
  dropped), and the **cancellation boundary** (`cancel` marks in-flight items
  cancelled and bumps their `revSeq` to the cancel `seq`, so pre-boundary
  stragglers drop while a post-boundary continuation still applies).
- `TranscriptEventView.tsx` — web/DOM renderer. Reuses the chat `parseSegments`
  pipeline for agent prose+code; renders each row from `item.kind`/`item.status`;
  `dir="auto"` bidi-resolves Unicode/RTL; the error row is a distinct
  `role="alert"` state (J4). No text/length inspection anywhere. Exposed as the
  `@elizaos/ui/native-transcript` subpath.

## Case-matrix conformance

`fixtures/native-transcript-golden.json` is the **language-neutral** golden
fixture (schema-tagged `{ schema, scenarios[] }`) so the iOS/Android native
decoders can load the exact same bytes and assert the same reduced model. The
conformance test decodes + reduces every scenario and deep-equals `expectView`.

Cases covered (golden fixture + focused unit tests): malformed, duplicated,
reordered, concurrent (interleaved turns), empty, Unicode/RTL, long, offline
(reconnect lost/restored), permission-denied (error), and cancellation boundary.

## Test results (web-verified, per-file vitest)

```
bunx vitest run src/native-transcript/    → 4 files, 42 tests passed
  contract.conformance.test.ts  18 passed   (golden matrix: decode + reduce)
  decode.test.ts                            (field-level rejections, stream guards)
  reduce.test.ts                            (dedupe no-op, late guard, terminal turn, long text)
  TranscriptEventView.test.tsx              (jsdom DOM: roles/status/dir/code/alert/speaking)
bunx tsgo --noEmit -p packages/ui          → EXIT 0 (no diagnostics)
```

## Web-verified vs device-gated (explicit)

- **Web-verified here:** contract, decoder, reducer, web renderer, and the full
  conformance matrix — all as fast per-file vitest (results above). Evidence
  screenshots/video are a lightweight standalone render of the component
  (`react-dom/server` → the real `parseSegments`+`CodeBlock`), since the renderer
  is not yet wired into a routed page (that integration is a separate UI task).
- **Device-gated remainder (NOT done here — needs a real iOS-sim / Android-emu
  pass):** the SwiftUI and Android-Views decoders that consume
  `native-transcript-golden.json`. No native transcript decoder is tracked on
  `develop` today (the earlier #16200 native work exists only in gitignored
  generated project dirs), so there was nothing to wire. This PR defines the
  contract + golden fixture so those decoders **can** conform; the fixture is the
  cross-language source of truth. I ran **no** iOS simulator or Android emulator
  and claim **no** native verification.

message with a lifted `js` code block + copy button, tool status row, RTL Arabic
+ emoji turn, a distinct permission-denied error alert, and the live speaking
indicator).

7c:T4029,Closes #16943

## What + why

The ratified home spec (`docs/design/NOTIFICATIONS-WIDGETS-SYSTEM.md` §B "Explicitly NOT residents" / §E item 3, decided in #14560, landed in #14669) demoted `wallet.balance` off the home surface — but only half the migration shipped: the home card was removed while **neither promised compensation existed**. `WalletBalanceWidget` was fully-implemented dead code with zero consumers, and the producer-side balance-delta notification was a comment pointing at a follow-up that never happened. Meanwhile `MVP.md`'s workstream-3 bar still demanded the opposite ("quiet home shows … **+ wallet**").

This PR records the decision — **the demotion stands** (option (a) in the issue) — reconciles the documents, and completes the migration.

### Part 1 — decision + doc reconciliation
- **Decision record** added to the spec's §E migration map ("Item 3 decision record (wallet-on-home, closed 2026-07-23)"), citing #14560 → #14669 → this PR.
- **`MVP.md`**: workstream-3 acceptance bar drops "+ wallet" from the quiet-home clause and restates the wallet criteria against the routed surface + producer notification; scope and status snapshots updated to match.
- **`research/03-launcher-widgets.md`**: dated "Superseded on wallet-on-home" status block; the stale "no test asserting the sparse resting state" claim corrected (`assertQuietHome` exists and is green).
- **`packages/ui/src/widgets/registry.ts`**: the stale "follow-up note in PR #14560" comment now points at the real producer.

### Part 2 — complete the migration
1. **Price surface mounted user-reachable** (`packages/ui/src/components/pages/WalletSectionNav.tsx`): the wallet section root (`/wallet`, `/inventory` alias — and only the root, not the Perps/Predictions sub-tabs) renders the real `WalletBalanceWidget`: BTC/SOL/ETH defaults for a confirmed-empty wallet, top-3 held ≥$1 by holding value, 60s visibility-gated refresh, three-state loading/unavailable/ready, **price-only #10706 invariant intact**. The previously-orphaned component gains its production consumer.
2. **Balance-delta notification producer** (`packages/agent/src/runtime/wallet-balance-delta.ts`): a **structural `kind: "watcher"` ScheduledTask** on the one scheduling spine — interval trigger (30 min), escalation step on a contributed dispatch channel, `respectsGlobalPause`, `bg-light-30s` profile. **No promptInstructions-driven behavior**: the fire is handled by a deterministic dispatcher that samples total wallet USD (same address/RPC-readiness/fetcher stack as `/api/wallet/balances`), compares against a persisted baseline, and on a material move (≥$10 AND ≥10% of baseline; structurally overridable via task `metadata.walletBalanceDelta`) emits a `category: "general"` notification through `NotificationService` (`groupKey: wallet:balance-delta` so repeats coalesce per §C.3, `deepLink: /wallet`, **percent-only lock-screen-safe copy** — dollar figures ride `data` for in-app consumers). Error doctrine: a failed balance read returns a typed retryable dispatch failure (never "balance is zero"); a change in *which* legs are samplable re-baselines silently instead of fabricating a delta; the baseline only advances after the notification is accepted. Registered from agent boot, desktop/cloud only (same gate as the `/api/wallet` routes).
3. **Generic seam in `@elizaos/plugin-scheduling`** (`registerScheduledTaskChannelDispatcher`): per-runtime contributed dispatch-channel registry, mirroring the in-tree coding-agent pr-shepherd recipe precedent. The runner host routes contributed channels at dispatch time (late registration honored) and folds contributed keys into `channelKeys()`/`channelAvailable` so schedule-time channel validation accepts them. No consumer imports enter the scheduling spine — dependencies still point inward.
4. **E2E so the surface cannot silently orphan again**: `run-wallet-widget-e2e.mjs` gains a wallet-section surface mode that mounts the REAL `WalletSectionNav` (real app-shell registry + tab strip) and asserts the price rows render on the decided surface at desktop and mobile viewports, plus jsdom specs (`WalletSectionNav.price-surface.test.tsx`).

7d:T2110,## Summary

Successor to #16656 (revived per the blocking reviews there; co-credited to @0xSolace / wakesync). Lands #15891's enabling move — a native-hosted wallpaper below the WebView so the chat sheet adopts real iOS 26 `UIGlassEffect` — **now proven live on an iOS 26.4.1 simulator** (see evidence), with the four architectural defects of the original design removed plus a fifth latent bug this PR's device lane uncovered:

1. **The iOS cookie disclosure is unexpressible, not just fixed.** The wallpaper crosses the bridge as **pre-downsampled base64 bytes the page already loaded** (canvas-flattened onto the wallpaper color at screen scale), never as a URL. Both native plugins contain **zero** network/cookie machinery — no `URLSession`, no `httpCookieStore`, no `CookieManager`, no bundled-asset heuristics — and `native-glass-backdrop-contract.test.mjs` gates the *absence* of that machinery. This also erases the double-fetch, the scheme allow-list, the localhost-dev hole, and the Android OOM risk (decode bounded to screen scale by the web encoder).
2. **Temporal scoping fixes the CSS-blur blindness.** CSS `backdrop-filter` can only sample WebView pixels, so a permanently-native wallpaper blinds every CSS glass surface in the app. The wallpaper is native **only while the sheet holds an anchor lease at rest**; the DOM paints it the rest of the time, `clearBackdrop` restores the opaque-WebView fast path, and shader/color sessions never touch the bridge.
3. **"Settled" means settled.** The anchor enables on `sheetSettled` (finger up **and** the release spring finished) — not pointer-up — so the region never chases the settle spring with per-frame `updateRect` churn. The handoff is acknowledgement-ordered both ways: wallpaper ack → region ack → one-commit flip; drag-start restores CSS on the same render.
4. **iOS-only anchoring.** Android's bridge panel is 96–99% opaque — per-sheet anchoring there is churn with a worse look than CSS over a DOM wallpaper (the #16200 finding). Android rendering is byte-for-byte unchanged; the byte-piped backdrop contract still ships there for future translucent materials.
5. **iOS GlassBridge was never actually registered.** Discovered by this PR's device lane: in-app Capacitor plugins never appear in the cap-sync-generated `packageClassList` and nothing called `registerPluginInstance` — so `isNativeGlassAvailable()` has answered **false on every iOS build since the parity PR**. One line in `ElizaBridgeViewController.capacitorDidLoad` fixes it, and the new observable diag channel (`chat-glass-diag:*` on the AX probe — the observable half of the tier system's J4 degrades) is what found it.

Also kept from the review findings: the 50% `--bg` legibility scrim stays DOM-painted on the native tier, and `applyRootCanvasPaint` is the single owner of root-canvas transparency.

## How it works

`AppBackground` passively publishes the resolved image-wallpaper source to `glass/native-backdrop.ts`. When the chat sheet settles open (`ChatOverlay` → `useNativeGlassAnchor`), the anchor encodes the wallpaper once (cached), pipes bytes natively (invisible — DOM still paints), attaches the glass region (still invisible), then flips the store — DOM wallpaper hides, root canvas goes transparent, and the sheet surface drops fill+blur in one React commit. Any drag returns CSS on that same render. Every refusal path (old shell, shader wallpaper, CORS-taint, Android, web, desktop, below iOS 26) stays on the exact current CSS tiers and reports WHY on the probe.

- `packages/ui` vitest: glass (16) + AppBackground (16) + backgrounds + blur-gate + **full ChatOverlay matrix — 280 tests green** post-instrumentation.
- `packages/app-core` native contract test (4) — including the no-network/no-cookies gate.
- **On-device XCUITest, PASSING** (`GestureSemanticsUITests/testChatSheetNativeGlassTierRestAnchoring`, iPhone 17 Pro sim, iOS 26.4.1, app built from this branch via `run-mobile-build.mjs ios-local`): settled HALF rest → `chat-glass-tier:native`; inset FULL rest → `native` re-anchor after gestures; probe channel hard-required. The over-pull-maximize sub-leg is best-effort on this simulator shape (the canonical `testChatMaximizeAndRestore` fails the same gesture commit there, pre-existing; the full-bleed css gate stays pinned by the jsdom matrix).

7e:T32ca,## What

Replaces the React-mounted LifeOps activity-signal hook with a lifecycle-safe, self-starting renderer service, implementing the architecture specified in #16504 (which supersedes the activation half of #16471 — the connector dependency half of #16471 already landed in #16423).

### Loader identity (packages/app)

- `discoverSideEffectAppModules` now emits a **role-qualified cache identity** `<package>#<mode>` (plus `packageName` and `mode` fields). The renderer's dynamic-import cache also holds package ROOT facades keyed by bare package name (`cachedDynamicImport` in `main.tsx`); a bare-name key would let whichever loader ran first suppress the other and hand consumers the wrong namespace (`packages/app/vite/app-side-effect-modules.ts`).
- `plugin-registrations.test.ts` now proves: role-qualified keys for every discovered module, no collision with any `main.tsx` shell cache key, and — running the **real production Vite transform** over the real `plugin-registrations.ts` — that the generated loader list imports Personal Assistant's `src/register.ts` entry, never its root facade.
- `route-coverage.test.ts` ratchets key on `packageName` so view-manifest bookkeeping is unaffected.

### Renderer scope (packages/ui)

- New `isPrimaryAppRenderer()` in `@elizaos/ui/platform` (window-shell.ts): the single predicate for "should a self-starting renderer service run here". Rejects `?shell=`/`?shellMode=` window shells, `?popout`, `?mode=companion` (phone companion), `?appWindow=1` app windows, and the `/model-tester` route (path and `file:` hash navigation). Unit-tested across all shell modes.

### Lifecycle-safe capture (plugin-personal-assistant)

- `src/lifeops/activity-signals-capture.ts`: imperative controller replacing the deleted `useLifeOpsActivitySignals` hook.
  - **Idempotent singleton**: a second start returns the active stop; a fresh session is only claimable after stop.
  - **Race-safe stop**: every awaited MobileSignals startup step re-checks the stop flag; a stop landing mid-startup disposes the late listener, halts monitoring, and installs no interval (`dispose()` in `finally` covers failed/interrupted startups).
  - **Teardown**: non-bfcache `pagehide` stops the capture; HMR dispose in `register.ts` stops it on module replacement.
  - **Observable failures**: only transport loss and the 503 runtime-starting shape read as "stand down"; any other status-probe or capture failure dispatches a `capture_error` status event instead of silently reading as not-ready (kept handlers annotated `error-policy:J4/J7`).
- `src/register.ts`: side-effect boot entry (`elizaos.appRegister: "register"` + `sideEffects` in package.json), gated on `isPrimaryAppRenderer()`, export-free by contract.
- Removed: `LifeOpsActivitySignalsEffect`, `src/hooks/useLifeOpsActivitySignals.ts`, the `main.tsx` Suspense mount, and the stale ambient declarations.
- Blocker settings cards now import leaf `@elizaos/ui` subpaths (the root barrel would drag the router/component tree into the facade chunk shared with the register path).

## Tests (real path, no mocks of the thing under test)

- `packages/app/src/pa-registration-entry.test.ts` — loads the PA root facade and register entry **together** (facade aliased to `src/ui.ts` exactly like the production build): each evaluates exactly once with its own namespace/exports, register self-starts the capture in a primary renderer, and starts nothing under `?popout=1`. Only test double is the jsdom URL.
- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/routes/lifeops-activity-signals.test.ts` — end-to-end against a real PGlite-backed `AgentRuntime`: HTTP-shaped POST → real route dispatcher → LifeOpsService → repository insert; the artifact is read back via GET **and** straight from `app_lifeops.life_activity_signals` SQL; invalid input is a 400 with no row.
- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/activity-signals-capture.test.ts` — 18 tests over the controller lifecycle: singleton/idempotency, stop-during-every-awaited-native-step races, failed-init cleanup, pagehide teardown, dedupe, mobile snapshot mapping, expected-vs-unexpected failure classification.
- `packages/ui/src/platform/window-shell.test.ts` — `isPrimaryAppRenderer` across every excluded shell mode.
- PA test-stub de-larp: `createIntegrationTelemetrySpan` and `checkRateLimit` in `test/stubs/agent.ts` now re-export the real implementations instead of shape-drifted fakes.

7f:T16f8,Refs #16888

## Summary

- add a dedicated direct-distribution LP3 Cloud debug build lane guarded by `ELIZA_ANDROID_LP3_COLOR_POLICY_ENABLED=1`;
- persist an explicit operator opt-in in device-protected private storage and maintain full color only on `Light` / `TLP301` devices with `WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS`;
- observe only the two daltonizer settings, debounce launcher rewrites, write mismatches only, verify readback, and stop observably on opt-out, permission loss, wrong device, or failure;
- keep every policy class, component, action, and restricted permission out of ordinary Cloud and release artifacts.

This does not touch `light_mode`, SurfaceFlinger, Qualcomm display properties, auth/app data, or networking.

## Safety boundary

- The implementation lives in an external debug-only source set. Canonical `app/src/main` contains no policy code or components.
- The build flag is accepted only by the dedicated `android-cloud-debug` direct-distribution command and is rejected for release, Play, SMS, AOSP, app-dir, sideload, and whitelabel targets.
- Runtime activation requires all of: compile-time lane enabled, exact `Light` / `TLP301`, granted privileged permission, and explicit same-UID operator opt-in.
- The service and receiver are unexported. Operator enable/disable/sync commands are same-UID only.
- The persistent foreground service uses the Android `specialUse` contract and a visible low-importance notification.

80:Td2c,## Summary

Fixes #16650.

A dedicated agent could keep process/container health green while its embedded PGlite was terminally closed, leaving all DB-backed operations dead until a manual restart. This PR makes database liveness an exercised end-to-end contract and gives the fleet supervisor a bounded recovery path.

- perform a real `SELECT 1` in runtime and cloud-agent health paths
- classify terminal PGlite closure separately from transient probe failures
- return HTTP 503 with `ready: false` and `canRespond: false` on terminal closure
- expose DB liveness in cloud-agent health and status RPC payloads
- consume terminal DB health in fleet heartbeat and enqueue the existing deduplicated restart job
- bound recovery to three restarts per one-hour window with a ten-minute cooldown
- keep transient failures on normal retry/hysteresis, with no immediate restart
- isolate the DB recovery budget from unrelated shared `error_count` uses
- make PGlite `isReady()` exercise the database rather than cached manager state

No auth, billing, backup/restore, persistence, or state-durability behavior is weakened.

## Incident proof

The regression test starts a real in-memory PGlite, proves `/api/health` is green, closes PGlite while the runtime remains alive, and proves health flips to HTTP 503 with terminal DB state. Supervisor tests prove terminal recovery enqueue, transient suppression, cooldown, and budget exhaustion.

# Risks

**Medium / CI enforcement.** This intentionally removes policy-only blockers,
so raw coverage percentages, historical file counts, inventories, and debt
baselines can no longer fail a PR. The risk is accidental loss of useful
behavior checks hidden inside mixed ratchet files; those checks were extracted
into direct tests and added to the develop-PR workflow.

This does **not** remove actual correctness, security, publish-safety,
accessibility, visual-regression, runtime performance, touch, memory, or live
output-quality tests.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Removes the remaining CI floors and ratchets that can fail independently of
product behavior:

- coverage percentage, per-file, and changed-source floors
- minimum task, issue, scenario, story, action, interaction, view, package,
  plugin, and command counts
- historical inventory, allowlist, debt, keyless-E2E, and story-play baselines
- blocking E2E manifest-gap and UI-determinism baselines
- blocking alias-read historical comparison

Coverage and E2E completeness tools remain available as diagnostic reports
where useful, but no longer act as ship gates based on counts.

Mixed files were not deleted blindly. Their real behavior assertions now live
in focused tests for:

- plugin view routing
- external API mock artifact integrity
- E2E resolution and larp-fixture detection
- runner failure classification
- widget registration

Develop PR CI now runs the owning-package and repository script tests
directly. Workflow documentation, retired scripts, and retired baselines are
updated together.

## Review follow-up commits

A full adversarial review of the sweep landed two follow-up commits:

- **`fix(ci)`** — the new develop-PR `test` job invoked
  `run-turbo.mjs run test --affected`, but the repo defines no Turbo `test`
  task (run-all-tests.mjs is the canonical orchestrator), so the job failed on
  every PR; `--affected` also resolves to all ~254 packages whenever the root
  `package.json` changes. The job now maps changed files to the workspace
  packages that own them and runs those suites through `run-all-tests.mjs`
  (root/global-input changes defer to the post-merge exhaustive lane). Also
  adds the `name: test` marker required by the aggregate workflow contract
  (`develop-pr-aggregate.self-test.mjs` failed at the original head), retargets
  a `test.yml` step that still named the long-deleted
  `plugin-tui-view-coverage.test.ts`, and records the
  `audit:e2e-coverage` → `report:e2e-coverage` rename in the migration table.
- **`test:`** — six checks deleted inside ratchet-named files were
  prohibitions or integrity assertions rather than count policy; they are
  restored baseline-free: the three scenario-corpus larp detectors
  (actionCalled-only / echo-satisfiable / entirely-skippable finalChecks),
  executor-ignored turn-assertion spelling rejection, the app boot-plugin
  packaged-dependency check, e2e inventory-scanner behavior tests, hard
  manifest artifact integrity, and chat-sidebar slot resolution mechanics.
  Wiring the corpus guards into the scenario-pr unit lane also removed the
  duplicate scenario-runner unit command the zero-key ownership report
  flagged.

83:Tc6b,Fixes #17106.

## Summary

This is the correctness repair for the connection-readiness cache introduced by #17074.

- preserves existing world metadata when another caller connects, so one caller cannot erase another caller's role grant;
- removes the successful-readiness cache and reconciles every turn before persistence or generation;
- serializes shared-world mutations while coalescing only identical in-flight work;
- fences every descriptor by exact runtime, topology, room generation, caller identity, and deletion state;
- makes room generations globally unique and safely evictable, so a stale descriptor cannot revive after deletion;
- applies a 15-second request-facing deadline without releasing the raw mutation lock: hung adapter I/O remains quarantined and later writes fail fast until it actually settles;
- awaits readiness before REST/SSE generation, rejects stale terminal success, and releases the client message id on every undelivered connection failure.

## Root cause

The old registry treated a completed ensure as permanent proof. The underlying world, room, runtime, caller role, or character name could change while the cache continued to say "ready." Concurrent callers also updated one shared world with read/replace writes, so a later connection could erase the first caller's role metadata. Moving refresh off the critical path let generation and callbacks run before that prerequisite was known to be valid.

The replacement treats connection reconciliation as a required turn prerequisite. It preserves raw mutation exclusivity even when the HTTP caller times out; it does not use a lock-releasing `Promise.race`.

## Validation on exact head

- 42 focused tests across the real in-memory adapter, readiness races, rename/delete/runtime replacement, idempotency, failure-kind, and full SSE contract;
- hung-I/O test proves timeout at 15 seconds, no second write starts, quarantine remains exclusive, and recovery occurs only after raw I/O settles;
- `packages/core` typecheck passed;
- Biome passed on all seven changed files;
- `git diff --check` passed.

# Risks

Low. The change is confined to OpenAI-compatible Cloud SSE translation. It
fails malformed provider tool-call streams closed with a terminal typed error;
text-only stream behavior and the non-streaming path are unchanged. The added
manual live-evidence mode is fail-closed to this exact same-repository branch,
trusted actor, and requested commit before any secret-bearing step can run.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

#16973 landed during review and is now this PR's baseline. It correctly stops
the AI SDK's consolidated event from duplicating argument text already emitted
as deltas. A residual remained: any nonempty fragment stream was accepted
without proving that it was complete or matched the consolidated provider input,
and its multi-call regression was sequential.

This PR tracks every streamed tool call independently by provider call ID,
preserves its stable OpenAI index/name, and appends argument fragments exactly
as they arrive. At the consolidated `tool-call` event, it proves that the
fragments form one complete JSON value and semantically match the provider's
consolidated input before treating the call as complete.

Consolidated-only and empty-delta providers still emit one complete argument
payload. Duplicate, out-of-order, malformed, truncated, mismatched, or
unfinished tool-call streams end with
`CHAT_TOOL_CALL_STREAM_INVALID` plus `[DONE]`, never a plausible partial success.
Observed `tool-input-end` and `finish` events also have an explicit ordering
contract: end-before-start, duplicate end, delta-after-end, duplicate finish,
and every event after finish fail closed. Once a call completes, the terminal
finish reason remains `tool_calls`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking transport correctness fix).

# Documentation changes needed?

No. This repairs the existing OpenAI-compatible streaming contract without
adding a public option or changing documented request/response schemas.

## Deployment instructions

No special steps beyond the normal Cloud API deployment pipeline.85:Tea6,## Why: every chat-latency regression is the same event
Every latency regression this project keeps re-fixing (this session alone: available_apps registry refetch 10.9s, cloud billing tail 1.6s, gateway balance re-read 300ms, FACTS union-find) is **a synchronous `await` (network/DB/`useModel`) added to the per-turn serving path** — and each shipped green because no guard caught it: below the 3s per-provider hard cap, outside composeState, logged only at debug, or orthogonal to the cloud structural locks. Turn latency is a shared budget spent by dozens of independently-owned components, and nothing fails a PR for spending it.

## The guards (each catches a distinct class)
1. **`audit:hot-path-latency-ratchet`** (`packages/scripts/hot-path-latency-ratchet.mjs`, wired into `verify` after `error-policy-ratchet`) — **the keystone.** Diff-scoped, modeled verbatim on `error-policy-ratchet.mjs`: fails a PR that *adds* a bare awaited `fetch`/DB-read/`useModel` inside a `Provider.get()` body or a hot-path manifest file (`runtime.ts` composeState, `message.ts`, `eliza-sandbox.ts`) **without** a sanctioned shape — `ctx.waitUntil(...)`, `Promise.race`/`withTimeout`, a cache-hit guard, a declared `timeoutMs`, or an inline `// latency-ok: <reason>` escape hatch. Immune to develop drift (only counts *increases* in *touched* files; no-op on develop / when the base ref is unresolvable). Would have flagged **all four** of this session's regressions at review time.
2. **provider purity tripwire** (`compose-state-provider-purity.test.ts`) — boots a real AgentRuntime with the real default provider bundles, patches `useModel` to throw + records `fetch`, composes once, asserts **zero `useModel` and zero external fetch during composeState** (with sanity asserts so an all-errored run can't vacuously pass).
3. **post-reply tail watchdog** (`inference-timing.ts`) — `logger.warn` (never throws) when a turn keeps working *after* the reply is delivered beyond `ELIZA_TURN_TAIL_BUDGET_MS` (default 500ms) — the shared-tier billing-tail class, made visible on every live turn.

_(The 4th guard — a default-budget case for `compose-state-provider-budget.test.ts` — rides with #16882 which owns that file.)_

86:T1119,## Summary

Durable turn / outbox state machine that closes the crash window **deliberately
left open by #16696**. #16696 suppresses a duplicate inbound Discord turn once
the inbound memory is persisted. That fixed double-dispatch but left a worse
gap: a crash **after inbound persistence but before the outbound reply** caused
permanent replay suppression, so the message was ingested and **no reply was
ever sent** (SOLIZA cutover charter rows **D4/D5**).

> ⚠️ **Stacked on #16696** (`sol/discord-inbound-idempotency`). Review the top
> commit only; base #16696 first. If #16696 merges, rebasing this onto `develop`
> is a no-op on the shared lines.

## Design

Add a durable turn record keyed by the Discord message id with an explicit
lifecycle:

```
RECEIVED ──▶ DISPATCHED ──▶ REPLIED   (terminal)
                    └──────▶ FAILED    (terminal, retries exhausted)
```

On redelivery / process restart, instead of blindly returning when the inbound
memory exists:

- **terminal (REPLIED / FAILED)** → genuine no-op
- **non-terminal + a reply memory already exists** → reconcile to REPLIED, **no
  resend**
- **non-terminal, no reply, retry budget remains** → **resume** the reply path
- **retry budget exhausted** → explicit terminal **FAILED** (no silent drop)

### Delivery model: send-then-record + reconcile probe (documented choice)

The connector already writes a durable **outbound reply memory** for every
message it emits (`content.inReplyTo = <inbound id>`,
`metadata.platformMessageId = <discord reply id>`). That reply memory **is** the
authoritative record-of-send. Rather than add a second "reserved-before-send"
outbox row that can itself desync from Discord on a crash, we **reconcile
against the reply memory the send path already produces**: before re-dispatching
a resumed turn we probe for an existing reply to this inbound; if one exists the
reply already went out (crash in the send↔record gap) and we reconcile to
REPLIED without resending. Bounded retries are safe because the send path is
content-idempotent via #15601's outbound reservation and #13054's outbound
memory dedupe.

The **#16696 pre-persistence retry semantics are preserved**: a failure before
the durable claim leaves nothing persisted and stays retryable.

## Validation

Focused Vitest, run **green locally** in the worktree (`bun install` + generated
i18n keywords):

```
✓ plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/turn-state.test.ts (11 tests)
✓ plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/messages-inbound-idempotency.test.ts (5 tests)
✓ plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/messages-durable-turn.test.ts (6 tests)
Test Files  3 passed (3)
     Tests  22 passed (22)
```

Full discord messages regression lane (65 tests) also green.

Crash-simulation coverage (mandated cases): (a) crash after persist before
dispatch → resume replies once; (b) crash after dispatch before reply record →
resume replies once, no double send; (b2) reply already delivered before crash →
reconcile, no resend; (c) redelivery after REPLIED → no-op; (d) bounded retry
exhaustion → terminal FAILED, no silent drop; plus pre-persistence failure stays
retryable.
