# Risks

Low, and it cannot touch production until an operator runs `install.sh` as root on a runner host. No runtime code changes; the tool itself (`prune-runner-workspaces.ts`, #15504) is unchanged. The scheduled invocation keeps the tool's built-in `Runner.Worker` active-job guard (never passes `--allow-active`), so a timer firing during a live CI job prunes nothing.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`prune-runner-workspaces.ts` (#15504) is intentionally host-local and documents "run from cron/systemd on the runner host" — but the repo shipped **no timer, cron, or deployment wiring**, so it never ran. That's why prod-2 (`eliza-prod-robot-2`, 98 GB root) refilled to 100% on stale runner `_work` checkouts (51 GB) while Docker cleanup found nothing to reclaim, and an operator cleared it by hand again on 2026-07-15 (per Shaw's triage).

Adds the missing recurring call site as a **system-level systemd bundle** at `packages/deploy/runner-workspace-cleanup/`:

- `units/eliza-runner-workspace-prune.service` — `Type=oneshot`, runs `bun <tool> --root <root> --min-age <hours>`; `ConditionPathExists=<runner root>` makes it a clean no-op on non-runner hosts; `Nice=10` + `IOSchedulingClass=idle` keep it out of the agents' way.
- `units/eliza-runner-workspace-prune.timer` — `OnBootSec=15min`, `OnUnitActiveSec=1h`, `Persistent=false`.
- `install.sh` — idempotent, **root** (the runners' `_work` is under a root-owned `/opt/actions-runners`, unlike the user-level bot bundle in `../systemd/`). Copies the **zero-dependency** tool standalone to `/opt/eliza-runner-workspace-cleanup/`, so the robot needs neither a full checkout nor `node_modules` — only `bun`. Tunables via env (`RUNNER_WORKSPACE_ROOT`, `PRUNE_MIN_AGE_HOURS`, `BUN_BIN`).
- `smoke-test.sh` — static contract check (renders units, asserts no unresolved tokens, asserts the guard is intact and `--allow-active` is absent, asserts a real recurring timer wired to `timers.target`, `bash -n` + `systemd-analyze verify` when available). No root, no install, no host writes.
- `README.md` — operator guide, tunables, verify/uninstall.

## What kind of change is this?

Ops/deployment wiring (no runtime behavior change; fills a missing scheduled call site).

# Documentation changes needed?

The bundle ships its own `README.md`. No other docs change required.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`smoke-test.sh` — it encodes the contract: the rendered service invokes the tool with the active-job guard intact (asserts no `--allow-active`), the expected `--root`/`--min-age`, and a real periodic timer bound to `timers.target`. Then `install.sh` (root-only, idempotent, copies the standalone tool + renders units + enables the timer).

## Detailed testing steps

None beyond the static smoke: this is deploy wiring, and the pruning logic is already covered by the tool's own `prune-runner-workspaces.test.ts` (unchanged). Ran locally: `shellcheck` clean, `smoke-test.sh` → `runner-workspace-cleanup smoke OK`. The live ops-apply + two-cycle recurrence proof on the hosts stay with @0xSolace per #15398.

N/A - no pixels or visual text changed.a7:Tc87,## Summary

- scope every send-side transcript mutation to its owning conversation, including optimistic rows, rAF-buffered tokens/status/tool events, terminal edits, replay, failures, and reconciliation
- persist a bounded pending-turn receipt so reload during send starts unlocked and either clears from server truth or restores the exact draft after 30 seconds
- distinguish lifecycle teardown from explicit Stop, preserving the receipt only across page teardown
- add deterministic regressions for mid-stream conversation switching, teardown recovery, explicit Stop settlement, and bounded composer restoration

Fixes #16658.

## Root cause

#10753 pinned the transport conversation at enqueue, but `useChatSend` still committed through one active `conversationMessages` state. A late callback owned by conversation A could therefore mutate whichever transcript was selected when the callback ran. Reload also discarded the in-memory optimistic row and cleared draft without a durable recovery token.

## Coordination / non-overlap

#16629 owns cold scope hydration. This PR does not touch its files or change auth, API-key validation, pinned pre-stream HTTP 402 billing, SSE auth ordering, or send prewarm/warm-path transport.

## Why

The prior full-flow report left about 50 ms labeled only as
`message-service-overhead`. That residual was too broad to diagnose, and the
existing detached post-delivery helper still contained a 30-second
`Promise.race` that did not cancel its task.

This change makes the remaining local work exact, removes a redundant room
read from the hot path, and adds a reproducible 30-turn live benchmark for the
fast Cerebras `gemma-4-31b` path.

## Runtime changes

- Splits the former message-service residual into exclusive
  `message-ingress`, `message-delivery`, `message-lifecycle`, and true residual
  `message-service-overhead` stages.
- Times incoming persistence, participant/room/mute resolution, attachment
  processing, pipeline hooks, outgoing persistence/events/callbacks, and run
  lifecycle bookkeeping individually.
- Resolves participant state and room concurrently.
- Reuses the preloaded primary room during mute resolution, removing the common
  duplicate room read without regressing timed or room-level mute behavior.
- Removes the 30-second post-delivery `Promise.race`. The task was already
  detached and the race never cancelled it; failures now surface through
  `runtime.reportError`.

## Live Cerebras benchmark

Adds `bun run --cwd packages/agent perf:cerebras-chat`, backed by a real
PGLite `AgentRuntime`, plugin-openai in Cerebras mode, production
`generateChatResponse`, streaming, persistence, delivery, and lifecycle
events.

The benchmark:

- pins `gemma-4-31b`;
- performs 3 warmups and 30 measured turns by default;
- requires a distinct `SPEED-S-N` proof from every final and streamed response;
- requires exactly one live LLM call per turn;
- writes raw prompts/outputs, usage, TTFT, first-visible, delivered/finalized,
  exclusive stage histograms, raw spans/flows, and provider/cache telemetry;
- fails fast when the Cerebras credential is missing;
- adds no artificial 30-second response timeout.

The manual `Cerebras Chat Flow Live` workflow uses the staging
`CEREBRAS_API_KEY`, runs the full-runtime benchmark and all-provider benchmark,
and uploads the exact JSON/log reports. It is intentionally dispatched from
`develop` after merge because new workflow files cannot be manually dispatched
until they exist on the default branch.

## Local all-provider telemetry

Real `AgentRuntime` + plugin-sql/PGLite, all 24 providers selected together,
3 warmups, 30 measured samples:

- fresh parallel compose: p50 **27.474 ms**, p95 **73.493 ms**, max
  **81.494 ms**
- max-cached compose: p50 **0.086 ms**, p95 **0.133 ms**, one isolated max
  **8.126 ms**
- cache coverage: **24/24 providers on every sample**
- effective parallelism: p50 **7.85x**, p95 **10.78x**

The fresh run was captured immediately after a full 253-workspace build and is
CPU/noise sensitive. The clean GitHub runner report will be the comparison
artifact. In this local sample the highest fresh p95 contributors were
ATTACHMENTS 67.384 ms, WORLD 62.498 ms, ACTIONS 55.949 ms, FACTS 39.412 ms,
RECENT_MESSAGES 39.267 ms, and DOCUMENTS 30.050 ms. Their overlap is why the
wall time is far below their sum.

a9:T16e0,Closes #15966
Closes #16485

## Problem

The OpenRouter adapter treated caller-provided Anthropic cache plans as best effort. Malformed entries could be silently filtered, capped before validation, duplicated through `Map` materialization, or ignored when no prompt segments existed. That changes a caller's cache/cost contract while still sending a successful request.

## Change

- Parse Anthropic-local cache options only for Anthropic model routes.
- Validate the complete breakpoint plan before applying the provider cap: array shape, non-negative integer indices, `ephemeral` type, `5m`/`1h` TTL, duplicates, segment bounds, and required `promptSegments`.
- Validate `maxBreakpoints` as an integer from 0 through Anthropic's four-block ceiling; reserve the appropriate slots for system/tools/trajectory markers.
- Throw typed `ElizaError` failures with code `OPENROUTER_INVALID_CACHE_BREAKPOINT` before any provider request instead of dropping malformed entries.
- Preserve caller OpenRouter/arbitrary provider options, strip adapter-local Anthropic planner fields from the wire, and keep attachment content when segmented prompt caching is active.
- Add a guarded real-API suite that sends two identical >1,024-token prefix requests through a real AgentRuntime and OpenRouter's Anthropic `claude-sonnet-4`, requiring a cache creation receipt followed by a cache read receipt. It also proves an unsupported TTL is rejected locally as the typed error.
- Keep the cache proof independent of structured-output parsing and use Node's built-in UUID generator in the pre-existing OpenRouter live suite.
- Replace the retired `x-ai/grok-2-vision-1212` image-description default with the active, image-capable `openai/gpt-4o-mini`; synchronize runtime metadata and docs, and pin the default at the handler boundary with a deterministic test.

ab:T128d,## What

Turn-scoped **single-flight DB read coalescing** on the composeState hot path — the same in-flight-shared memo pattern already shipped in #16912 (identity-cluster memo):

- **`getRoom`** was a bare `adapter.getRoomsByIds([roomId])` with no cache, fetched **~4× per compose** (RECENT_MESSAGES, entities, PLATFORM_CHAT, PLATFORM_USER each independently read the same room). Now served once per turn from a ~1s in-flight-shared memo, invalidated on every room mutation.
- **The compose-shape messages scan** (`tableName='messages'`, roomId, newest-first) is coalesced so concurrent providers requesting different limits share one superset fetch, **sliced exactly**.
- **ATTACHMENTS** now gates before fetching — skips the ~451ms room-attachments query on text-only turns.

New `packages/core/src/runtime/single-flight-memo.ts` (short-TTL, in-flight-shared promise memo, rejection self-evicting), copying `identity-clusters.ts:15-25`.

## Why

Real `InferenceTiming` on a simple DM turn (local mirror of a dedicated Eliza Cloud agent — same `@elizaos/core` runtime + model): composeState is **~250–455ms**, a 12-provider fan-out that **serializes on its slowest DB-backed providers** — ATTACHMENTS (451ms), RECENT_MESSAGES (388ms), FACTS (309ms) — with the room fetched 4× and the messages scan run more than once. On a **remote Postgres** container each duplicate round-trip also pays network RTT, so the win scales with DB latency.

**Scope (honest):** this attacks the **composeState DB slice only** (~200–400ms), not the whole ~2.5s pre-first-token turn — the bulk of that is the single `RESPONSE_HANDLER` model call, a separate (and currently parked) concern. Framing this as the DB-path lever, not the whole latency fix.

## Correctness

- **Zero prompt-content change**, no message-loop semantics touched.
- Every room/message **mutation wrapper invalidates** the memo synchronously after the adapter write, so a just-sent message / just-created room is never served stale — including `ensureConnection`, which writes via `adapter.upsertRooms` directly and is now explicitly invalidated.
- The messages-scan memo admits **only the exact newest-first room-scoped shape**; any narrowing param (entityId/agentId/worldId/unique/offset/end/metadata/textContains/asc/`includeEmbedding`/accessContext) bypasses it.
- **Contract note:** on a gate-fail (text-only) turn, `state.data.providers.ATTACHMENTS.data.attachments` is now `[]` rather than the fetched room attachments. No in-repo consumer reads that field (the ATTACHMENT read action re-fetches); flagging for any out-of-tree plugin.

## Tests

- New `turn-read-coalescing.test.ts` — **11 pass** (coalescing, mutation invalidation, the `ensureConnection`-bypass invalidation, TTL bound, exact-slice semantics against both adapter contracts).
- Adjacent compose / message / connection suites — **32 pass**.
- **Full core suite: 4,089 pass** (the 10 errors are pre-existing identically on base — a fake-runtime `reportError` gap in `message.credit-exhaustion-reply.test.ts`, not introduced here).

## Evidence

- **Real `InferenceTiming` / trajectory:** the composeState decomposition above (local mirror, `GET /api/dev/inference-timing`).
- **Harness** `perf-bench/compose-bench.ts` (real AgentRuntime + InMemoryDatabaseAdapter with a 20ms-latency serializing queue modeling single-threaded PGlite): baseline 7–8 serialized round-trips/compose collapse under the fix.
- **Live-mirror before/after cutover probe — N/A in this PR:** it requires rebuilding dists + restarting the live bot (a deploy/cutover step, not PR-CI). The local-mirror + harness numbers are the evidence here; the live wall-clock delta is a prediction pending cutover.

ac:T8de,## Summary

Fixes the Local Inference Bench manifest preflight by aligning every consumer with the bundle architecture slugs actually published in `elizaos/eliza-1` (`e2b` and `e4b`). Pending tiers now fail locally before any network request instead of producing misleading Hugging Face 404s.

The change also establishes one shared published-tier contract across the catalog, runtime downloader, UI downloader, manifest validator, and CI preflight. The runtime and Python publisher now agree on required native kernels, while drafter-backed MTP remains a structural bundle requirement.

Closes #15976.

ae:T3762,Fixes #17059 (recap "Stage-1 routing regression", variant B + Audit C planned-reply guard bypass).

## What

Variant A of #17059 (CLI agentic flip) was already fixed on develop (29d47580b99, `--tools ""`). This PR closes the two remaining defects, all in `packages/core`:

1. **Read-side empty-claim guard** — new `core.simple_empty_tracked_state_claim` response-handler evaluator beside the write-side `core.simple_completed_side_effect_claim`. A simple-path turn runs NO tools, so a reply asserting the user's tracked work is empty/absent ("your task list is empty", "no notes, tasks, or messages from earlier today", "I don't have today's log" — the byte-exact #17058 run 729acaf2 fabrication) is a fabricated empty day by construction. It reroutes to the planner whenever a registered action declares a tracked-work context (`tasks`/`todos`/`goals`/`reminders`/`followups`/`productivity`) — "not loaded must never read as zero" applied to reads. Detector lives in `services/message/side-effect-claims.ts` next to the write-side patterns; questions, conditionals, and non-task chat pass through.
2. **Routing vocabulary** — the compact DM Stage-1 catalog renders `descriptionCompressed` only, and the old compressed `tasks` line carried zero recap/status vocabulary, so "recap my day" had nothing to route on and fell to `contexts=["simple"]`. Both the compressed and full `tasks` descriptions in `runtime/default-contexts.ts` now carry recap/status/summary vocabulary ("recap my day", "what's left today", "what did I get done today").
3. **Recall-exception carve-out** — `current_turn_boundary`'s visible-context-recall exception taught answering recall-shaped asks from the visible window; tracked-work recaps (todos/habits/goals/day status) are now explicitly carved out as live tasks lookups, never answered (or fabricated empty) from the visible window.
4. **Planned-reply egress guard (Audit C)** — a bare planner REPLY is a control action (`canonicalPlannerControlActionName`): its text ships as `finalMessage` through `createV5ReplyStrategyResult` WITHOUT executing the REPLY action, so a fabricated "you're all set" bypassed both enforcement points (the `reply.ts` executed-REPLY guard and the Stage-1 evaluator). `evaluatePlannedReplyEgress` now re-applies both claim detectors at the planned_reply chokepoint (precedent for the single egress chokepoint: `runtime/__tests__/pii-swap-reply-egress.test.ts`), keeps the grounded-by-successful-tool exemption (control actions never ground), bounces with ONE corrective planner re-run under a forced non-terminal tool call, and ships an honest "not done / could not check" statement if the re-run still fabricates.

Also demotes catalog row `adhd-followthrough-end-of-day-wins-first-recap` to `authored` citing the #17058 failing runIds (d0e1092e, 24ea8eb8, 729acaf2), per #17059's first acceptance criterion, and records the post-fix re-verification runs below. Only that row is touched — the 16 A2 `live.adhd.follow.*` bench-surface rows belong to the lifeops-bench lane.

af:Tfac,## Compaction handoff generator (Charter row M4)

The **compaction / handoff-regeneration** side of sovereign memory. Siblings merged today:
- #16700 canonical file boot (read identity from files)
- #16702 canonical memory writeback (append per-fact daily memory with provenance)

Where writeback records *individual* facts as they happen, a **handoff** is the periodic *summary* the next session reads first. This PR adds the missing piece: a generator that turns a long session transcript into a **bounded** handoff and rotates it **atomically**.

### What it does
- **Preserves every seeded decision / todo / open-thread.** Extraction is deterministic (marker-based: `DECISION:`, `TODO:`/`- [ ]`, `OPEN THREAD:`, plus `[bracket]` variants), so nothing load-bearing is silently dropped by a flaky model call. Each item carries provenance (turn index + role + optional timestamp).
- **Stays bounded.** The handoff is what every future boot pays for, so it is size-capped (default 16 KiB). Preserved carry-forward items are **never** truncated; only the free-form recent-context tail is trimmed (oldest-first) to fit. If the preserved set alone exceeds the cap we surface it rather than lose state.
- **Rotates atomically.** Reuses #16702's `writeFileAtomic` (same-dir temp + rename). A crash between temp-write and rename leaves the **old or new complete** file, never a torn one.
- **File firewall.** All writes go through #16702's `authorizeWrite` — deny-by-default, confined to the approved write root, `HANDOFF.md` already allowlisted.
- **Idempotent-ish.** Re-compacting **replaces** rather than appends, so the handoff never grows unbounded even when the prior handoff is fed back in.

### Diff
- `compaction-handoff.ts` — pure `buildHandoffDocument` (no I/O, testable) + `generateAndWriteHandoff` (authorize → build → atomic write).
- `compaction-handoff.test.ts` — synthetic 200-turn fixtures only (no real memory touched).
- `canonical-memory-writeback.ts` — **one line**: `export` on `writeFileAtomic` so M4 can reuse it. No behavior change; #16702's 10 tests stay green.

### Tests (synthetic fixtures, file firewall)
- 200-turn synthetic session with N seeded decisions/M todos/K open-threads → handoff contains all of them, with provenance.
- Bracket + checkbox marker variants recognized; duplicates de-duped keeping first provenance.
- Size stays under cap with 400 padding turns of 300-char filler (tail trimmed, carry-forward intact).
- Never drops load-bearing items even when the preserved set alone exceeds an absurdly small cap.
- Atomic rotation: simulated `kill -9` (mocked `rename` rejection) between temp-write and rename leaves the **old complete** file, no temp sidecar; successful rotation yields the **new complete** file.
- Idempotent-ish: byte-identical regeneration; re-feeding the prior handoff does not duplicate items or balloon size.

`compaction-handoff.test.ts`: **12/12 green**. `canonical-memory-writeback.test.ts`: **10/10 green** (unchanged).

No workflow edits. No denylisted ports.

Co-authored-by: wakesync <shadow@shad0w.xyz>

b0:Taea,## Summary
- start the agent org lookup immediately after authoritative API-key validation, overlapping it with user/organization hydration
- preserve session-auth ordering and preserve the hydrated user's organization as the final scope authority
- prewarm the exact authenticated shared-agent status gate when the selected Cloud agent base becomes active in the chat UI
- keep send-time auth, pre-stream HTTP errors, and the canonical HTTP 402 billing contract unchanged

## Root cause
The instrumented strict-cold browser turn spent 9,227 ms in `resolveSharedAgent`, serializing API-key validation, user/org hydration, and agent resolution over a cold Worker/Hyperdrive path. The model bridge was only 1,259 ms. This change moves the independent agent read alongside user/org hydration once the key itself has passed validation, and uses agent selection as an early signal to hydrate that same scope before Send.

## Safety
- API-key validation remains first and authoritative
- user active, organization active, expiry, and membership checks are unchanged
- a stale key organization id falls back to a lookup using the hydrated user's organization, preserving prior scope semantics
- prewarm is best-effort only; send still executes the complete authoritative gate
- no SSE headers move ahead of auth or billing, and HTTP 402 behavior is untouched

## Validation
- Biome passed on all five changed files
- `git diff --check` passed
- focused Workers API-key tests passed, including a synchronization test proving org lookup starts while user hydration remains blocked
- UI focused suite could not start locally because the available sparse-worktree dependency cache lacks React; CI is authoritative for the two small prewarm helper tests

## Staging acceptance
Staging only. After deploy, run the real Chromium cold click-to-paint probe and record `scope`, `body`, `parse`, and `bridge` server timings. Target remains cold click-to-painted text under approximately 3 seconds.

## SIWE staging observation
The nonce 503 is independently reproducible at the currently deployed staging commit and is not coupled to this diff. Both staging hosts expose `x-ratelimit-policy: redis-unavailable`; the nonce route uses a per-request `SocketRedis` client from `REDIS_URL`, not the bound `CACHE_KV`, because nonce consumption requires atomic `GETDEL`. The same endpoint can transiently succeed and then take about five seconds before returning `nonce_storage_unavailable`, consistent with the already-documented unreliable Cloudflare Worker to Railway public TCP path. This predates #16619 (the nonce route last changed in #16258) and needs an atomic Worker-native nonce store or reliable REST Redis binding rather than an unsafe KV get/delete fallback.

# Risks

Medium, in the intended direction: the snapshot lane goes **fail-closed by default** (the issue's explicit ask — the lane must soak in staging before production eligibility). Operators enable with `ELIZA_SNAPSHOT_JOBS_ENABLED=true`; gated rows wait as `pending` and drain the moment the lane is enabled (the stale sweep deliberately keeps flipping stuck rows — DB-only, no hydration). Every other lifecycle lane is pinned independently operable. Hydration budgets are env-overridable for the soak.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

**Root cause found:** hydration buffered the entire snapshot body unbounded (`await res.json()`), then re-stringified it just to measure size — doubling peak memory per snapshot, with no lane switch and no way to keep a restart from resurrecting snapshot jobs. Implements each required behavior:

- **One explicit gate** — `agent_snapshot` claiming AND startup recovery are disabled unless `ELIZA_SNAPSHOT_JOBS_ENABLED` is exactly `"true"`; a dispatch-level belt re-schedules any snapshot job claimed through another path without burning an attempt (observable warn, never fabricated success).
- **Bounded hydration, metadata-validated before retention** — the body is STREAMED against a raw byte budget (bytes past it are never retained; the connection is cancelled); malformed JSON is fail-closed; expanded file-count/byte budgets cover `workspaceFiles` plus every manifest file set, counting `max(declared size, decoded base64)` so a lying manifest cannot under-declare; all validated before anything is persisted. The measured `size_bytes` comes from the counted stream — the re-stringify (and its memory doubling) is gone.
- **Sequential, settled phases** — the lane is forced to batch 1 when enabled; the per-job timeout + the fetch's own AbortSignal keep timed-out work from surviving detached.
- **Fail-closed observability** — every rejection is an explicit error naming the breached budget; no partial restore, no healthy-empty.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (production-safety; fail-closed by explicit design of the issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`provisioning-jobs-snapshot-gate.test.ts` (exact-match gate; claim exclusion with every other lane still claimed; batch forced to 1; restart-cannot-resurrect with gate off / can with gate on), then the hydration budget cases in `eliza-sandbox.test.ts` (a streamed oversized body rejected mid-stream and never retained; within-budget passthrough; file-count and expanded-byte fail-closed pre-retention). The dispatch suite arms the gate per snapshot case, so the execution path's success/failure/skip semantics are unchanged when enabled.

## Detailed testing steps

None: Automated tests are acceptable.

b2:T143e,## What

Adds a **canonical file boot** mechanism so a self-hosted, single-tenant Eliza runtime can boot its identity by reading the operator's real hand-authored files directly, instead of depending on a separately maintained (and silently drifting) compressed character JSON (`ELIZA_AGENT_CHARACTER_JSON`) as the source of truth.

At boot the composer:
1. resolves an allowlisted **manifest** of files — either a default set under `ELIZA_CANONICAL_BOOT_ROOT` (SOUL.md, IDENTITY.md, AGENTS.md, USER.md, TOOLS.md, memory/SYSTEM.md, the current handoff, awareness, playbook, channel guide) or an explicit JSON manifest via `ELIZA_CANONICAL_BOOT_MANIFEST`;
2. reads each file **READ-ONLY**, computes a **sha256** of the exact bytes, and logs `<sha256>  <label>` so the boot log is an auditable content record;
3. **fails loudly** (throws, aborting boot) if a file marked `required` is missing or empty — a sovereign identity must not silently fall back to a default preset;
4. appends the exact file contents, wrapped in labeled delimiters, onto the primary agent's `system` prompt so the model receives them verbatim.

## Why

A JSON shadow copy of the character drifts out of sync with the real files and gives no boot-time proof that what the model sees matches disk. Reading + hashing the files at boot makes the identity provable and eliminates the hand-maintained JSON as the source of truth.

## Design

- New module `packages/agent/src/runtime/canonical-file-boot.ts`: `resolveCanonicalManifest`, `composeCanonicalBootContext` (sha256 audit + loud required-file failure), `applyCanonicalFileBootToConfig`.
- Wired into `startEliza` right after `applySandboxCharacterFromEnv`, before `buildCharacterFromConfig`.
- Generic (no operator-specific paths hardcoded). **Inert** when neither env var is set, so every existing runtime is unaffected.
- Reads files read-only. Does **not** manage the daily/handoff write root (separate controlled-write concern).

## Tests

`packages/agent/src/runtime/canonical-file-boot.test.ts` — 12 tests, **synthetic fixtures only** (file firewall; no real personal file content is read or embedded). Covers manifest resolution (default + explicit JSON), sha256 correctness, required-missing / required-empty loud failure, a **canary** proof that a changed line in an allowed file reaches the composed boot context (with a changed sha256), and a tmpdir real-disk round-trip.

```
✓ packages/agent/src/runtime/canonical-file-boot.test.ts (12 tests)
✓ packages/agent/src/runtime/build-character-config.test.ts (4 tests)  # unchanged, still green
Test Files  2 passed (2)
     Tests  16 passed (16)
```

Format + lint clean (biome).

b3:Tcc5,## What

Extends #16714's bounded-retention mechanism from the memories/embeddings partitions to the `logs` table, empirically the single biggest always-growing surface (~3.9 log rows per memory row, ~3.4KB/row).

- Reuse the pure planner (`planRetention`/`policyIsActive`) unchanged.
- Generalize the config resolver: `resolveRetentionConfigWithPrefix` reads the same four bound keys under a caller-chosen prefix; `resolveRetentionConfig` delegates to it (no behavior change to memory retention).
- New `LogsRetentionService` (`eliza_logs_retention`): scans via `getLogs`, plans with the shared planner, deletes via `deleteLogs`. Independent `ELIZA_LOGS_RETENTION_*` config, **OFF by default**, unref'd timers, re-entrancy guard, restart-safe (no persisted cursor), shared per-sweep delete budget. Null-room logs bucket together so the count bound still applies. Registered in `eliza-plugin.ts` + exported from `index.ts`. Adapter narrowed via a structural type-guard rather than an unsafe cast.

## Scope

Logs ONLY. `central_messages` + `memory_access_logs` were also flagged unbounded, but the core IDatabaseAdapter exposes no clean get-by-time + delete-by-id pair for them, so bounding them cleanly needs new adapter methods; intentionally deferred.

## Config (all optional; absent => bound disabled)

```
ELIZA_LOGS_RETENTION_DAYS
ELIZA_LOGS_RETENTION_MAX_ROWS_PER_ROOM
ELIZA_LOGS_RETENTION_MAX_DELETE_PER_SWEEP
ELIZA_LOGS_RETENTION_INTERVAL_MINUTES   # default 360 (6h)
```

b5:Ta75,## What

A Playwright ui-smoke gate that measures **Shadow's actual stopwatch**: tap-send → first VISIBLE rendered assistant token in the browser, through the production SSE parser (`client-base.ts`) + streaming-commit rAF throttle (`useChatSend.ts`) + React render.

## Why

The server chat-latency harness (`packages/scripts/cloud/chat-latency.mjs`) measures first SSE **BYTE** on a warm, reused conversation. That number reported ~0.4–1.3s GREEN while Shadow's stopwatch read ~4s. First-byte is structurally blind to:
1. **Client render cost** — the byte lands, but nothing is on screen until the SSE is parsed, the token committed to React state, and a frame paints.
2. **Streaming shape** — a single-chunk `done` frame (non-incremental `generateText` on the bridge) delivers first-byte just as fast, but the user stares at a spinner until the whole reply pops in at once. First-byte cannot see that regression.

See telemetry inventory + measured breakdown: `projects/eliza-fleet/LATENCY-E2E-2026-07-22.md`.

## The gate (non-vacuous)

- Measures **first-render** (Shadow's felt first-token) in a real Chromium page via an in-page MutationObserver, `performance.now()` from the send click.
- **FAILS if streaming regresses to a single chunk** (asserts ≥5 chunks spread over ≥100ms) — the biggest UX lever.
- Two scenarios matching the pacing Shadow hit: **cold conversation first turn** + **second turn after a 35s idle gap**.
- Per-turn uniqueness handled by a fixture `⟦tN⟧` nonce so the same reply body is reused without confusing the first-render probe.
- Budgets deliberately generous (noisy CI runner): first-render < 1500ms with wide margin over the healthy path.

## Measured on develop (healthy path)

| Turn | first-render | full-render | streaming |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (cold) | 731ms | 1153ms | 22 chunks / 423ms |
| 2 (post-idle) | 679ms | 1089ms | 22 chunks / 409ms |

p95 first-render 731ms (budget 1500ms). Client render adds only ~80–130ms over first-token arrival, and the reply streams incrementally — proving the client path is healthy. Shadow's felt 4s is server-side pre-header + retry stalls (targeted by the merged #16629/#16713/#16743/#16763/#16785 fixes), which this gate now protects against client/streaming regression.

## CI wiring

Auto-discovers into the keyless `scenario-pr.yml` lane (`app-browser-auto-discovered` job) — **no workflow-file edit required**. Coverage ratchet (`test/ui-smoke-coverage.test.ts`) stays green (6/6). Deny-list valid. Biome clean.

## Scope

Zero production code touched — adds `chat-latency-e2e.spec.ts` + `lib/chat-latency-kpi.ts` only.

b7:Tb3f,## What
Deliver the assistant reply to the user **before** persisting the response memory. Previously the message loop `await`ed `createMemory(responseMemory)` and then called the delivery callback — so the user waited on a DB write before seeing the reply. This moves the persist off the reply critical path (delivered first, persisted after), while guaranteeing the persist still runs and its failures are observed.

## Why
Measured with real `InferenceTiming` on a live agent: the post-model `delivery` window was **~250–440ms** of awaited `createMemory` sitting *before* the user-facing callback. Same class as the merged shared-tier billing-tail defer (#16925) — take the non-user-facing work off the response path.

## Correctness (the delicate part — deliberate)
- **Persist still always runs** — deferred, not dropped; a persist failure is surfaced via `runtime.reportError`/logger, never swallowed.
- **Callback throw doesn't lose the memory** — ordering handled so a delivery error still persists.
- **Downstream ordering preserved** — anything that depends on the response memory existing (evaluators, MESSAGE_SENT emission) still awaits it; only the user-facing callback moves earlier.
- **No stale next-turn read** — the persist completes within the turn (deferred just past the callback), so an immediate follow-up's RECENT_MESSAGES sees the reply.

## Evidence — live battery on this exact code (local runtime, cloud gemma-4-31b)
12/12 normie prompts answered correctly, and the timing spans show the delivery callback firing **after** the model with the persist deferred. Simple turns 1.3–2.0s wall; web/live-info 2.7–4.4s; eliza-code write-and-run works. `message.deliver-then-persist.test.ts` added (real AgentRuntime + InMemoryDatabaseAdapter): asserts callback-before-persist, persist-still-completes, callback-throw-still-persists, and next-read-sees-reply.

b8:Tfd5,Closes #16831

## Summary

- authenticate every `plugin-app-control` `/api/views` loopback request with the configured API Bearer token
- keep canonical alias-aware `ELIZA_API_TOKEN` precedence and the existing `ELIZA_API_AUTH_TOKEN` compatibility fallback
- cover `listViews`, `getCurrentView`, `ViewsClient.navigate`, and the direct `VIEWS/show` navigation path
- preserve tokenless local development by omitting `Authorization` when neither token is configured

## Root cause

The view client and the direct show-navigation helper sent only `Content-Type`. Cloud and mobile runtimes that require local auth therefore returned HTTP 401 even while the user session and subsequent chat traffic remained healthy. The agent then paraphrased the tool failure as an authentication error.

## Real boundary proof

`views-loopback-auth.integration.test.ts` opens a real ephemeral TCP listener with a Bearer-protected `/api/views` surface. It proves:

1. the same route rejects a raw tokenless request with 401;
2. all three `ViewsClient` routes cross the boundary with the exact canonical token;
3. the action's separate `navigateToView` route crosses it with the compatibility token;
4. no token appears in a request URL or body;
5. no empty `Authorization` header is synthesized.

# Risks

Low, and deliberately asymmetric: the clear fires ONLY when the pairing-token mint itself answers 401/403 — proof of staleness. Every other failure keeps the token: fetch throws and 5xx fold to reason `error` (an offline PWA relaunch is the exact scenario the durable key was built for, PWA-SESSION-PERSIST-2026-07-18), the first `/api/auth/me` 401 merely *triggers* recovery (clearing there would defeat #15132's transparent re-pair), and the SHOW_WALL branches (no steward token, non-cloud server) say nothing about the pair token's validity. The negative cases are pinned by tests.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

**#16620 gave the pair token a durable write channel but never a delete channel.** Repo-wide, nothing removed `eliza:cloud-pair:api-token` from either storage. Boot adoption re-adopts the key on every launch and re-stamps it into two more persistent stores (active-server record, agent profiles). After a credential rotation or agent re-provision the app looped forever: dead token → 401 → recovery mint 401 → auth wall → next boot re-adopts the same dead token.

- **New `cloud-pair-token.ts` module** — `clearCloudPairApiToken()` empties BOTH storages the write channel targets. Storage-scoped by design: the live bearer/boot-config are untouched so in-flight requests survive; the wall renders next and the next boot finds nothing to re-adopt. A dedicated module (not a line in the persistence grab-bag) because sign-out/unpair/delete flows will need the same clear.
- **Wired at exactly one seam** — the recovery runner's `unauthorized` branch (mint answered 401/403), as an injectable dep mirroring the existing `persistPairApiToken` pattern. The default also scrubs the two at-rest copies boot adoption stamps (`scrubPersistedActiveServerToken`, `scrubPersistedAgentProfileTokens`), so the purge leaves no copy behind.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (permanent auth dead-loop after credential rotation on installed PWAs).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

- `agent-session-recovery-runner.test.ts` — the 401 case now asserts the purge fired; new cases pin 403 (purges), a fetch throw (reason `error`, NO purge — the offline-relaunch guarantee), and a 5xx (NO purge).
- `cloud-pair-token.test.ts` — the clear empties both storages, leaves unrelated keys, is a safe no-op when absent, and pins that the delete channel targets the exact literal the write channel exports (rename-drift guard).

## Detailed testing steps

None: automated tests are acceptable. Vitest lane: **13 pass / 0 fail** across the two changed suites. Changed-file line coverage from the changed-test lane: `cloud-pair-token.ts` 100%, `agent-session-recovery-runner.ts` 75.9% — both above the 50% floor.

# Evidence Details

The seam choice is the load-bearing decision: the runner is the ONLY place that distinguishes "the mint itself refused (401/403 → reason `unauthorized`)" from "the mint could not be reached (throw/5xx → reason `error`)". Clearing anywhere upstream (first `/api/auth/me` 401, the hook's generic failure path) would either defeat #15132's transparent re-pair or nuke a valid token on an offline relaunch. The runner already had the injectable-dep pattern (`persistPairApiToken`) — the clear mirrors it.

## Known gaps / failures

Explicit sign-out/unpair/agent-delete clearing stays open on #16666: the steward JWT and the pair token are distinct credentials, and a hub-level clear (`clearStaleStewardSession`) would wipe a still-valid pair token on mere steward-session expiry. That deserves its own decision + PR (parts touch rendered .tsx surfaces). The (low) boot-resolver origin/platform gate is likewise left on the issue.

ba:Tcb0,# Overall Project Weekly Summary (Jul 19 - 25, 2026)

## Executive Summary
This week, the ElizaOS team focused on hardening the framework’s infrastructure to ensure long-term stability and production readiness. By optimizing cloud performance and refining how agents manage memory and identity, we have significantly improved the reliability of our core services.

### Key Strategic Initiatives & Outcomes

**Strengthening System Reliability and Performance**
_Goal: We aimed to make the platform more stable and efficient by streamlining how our services handle data and cloud resources._
- Improved real-time voice reliability by implementing smarter resource management and reducing delays in speech processing ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
- Reduced system "cold-start" times and memory usage, ensuring the platform remains responsive as agent activity grows ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
- Standardized our development tools and fixed automated testing pipelines to ensure a more consistent and error-free experience for developers ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).

**Enhancing Agent Autonomy and Memory**
_Goal: We wanted to ensure agents can operate independently for longer periods without performance degradation._
- Introduced smarter memory management that prevents agents from storing unnecessary data, keeping them efficient over time ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
- Simplified how agents are set up, allowing operators to define agent identities directly through simple files ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
- Added new capabilities for agent reminders, enabling agents to proactively manage tasks and notifications for users ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).

## Repository Spotlights

### elizaos/eliza
- Migrated to updated development tools (TypeScript 7.0.2 and Biome 2.5.5) to ensure code quality and build consistency ([#16655](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16655), [#16729](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16729)).
- Stabilized the automated release pipeline by fixing unit test failures and improving the software publication process ([#16642](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16642), [#16307](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16307)).
- Optimized voice services by implementing atomic quotas and isolating chat workers to prevent system bottlenecks ([#16654](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16654), [#16643](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16643)).
- Introduced bounded memory retention to prevent agents from consuming excessive system resources ([#16714](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16714)).
- Enabled "canonical file boot," allowing for faster and more reliable agent identity initialization ([#16700](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16700)).
- Integrated end-to-end support for agent-driven reminders and task routing ([#16746](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/16746)).
- Resolved critical bugs affecting system recovery and user authentication, including issues with magic link codes and server-side data processing
